# Changelog

Semantic versions. A rename or a removed prop is a major step once the package reaches 1.0; until
then it is a minor step, and it ships with a deprecation the lint fixes (`ledger/no-deprecated-name`,
`ledger/no-deprecated-token`) wherever one is possible. Every entry names the story that shows it.

## Unreleased · audit implementation

- **`ledger/no-inline-config`: a comment does not switch a ledger rule off** (lint hardening 1). A configuration comment that sets a ledger rule (`/* eslint ledger/no-margin: "off" */`) and a block disable of one reach the rest of the file, so both are reported even with a reason; a line or next-line disable keeps one site and says why after `--`. A next-line disable that names no rule, bare or with a list of only commas or empty quotes (`// eslint-disable-next-line ,`), is reported too, and a comment is read as ESLint decodes it, so a key written with an escape (`"ledger\u002fno-margin"`) still names its rule. It is an error in both presets, stories and `bleed.tsx` included. Both lint configs set `reportUnusedDisableDirectives: "error"`, so a disable that silences nothing fails the run; the one in the chart's marks is gone. In this repository `scripts/check-allow-lists.mjs` counts every inline directive that names a ledger rule, and every disable that names none, per file, and the counts may only shrink; it fails when the root has an `eslint-suppressions.json` or a suppressions file carries a ledger rule, and it takes a key under a rule as that rule landing only when the rule is new to both the plugin and the base ref's Lint rules page. A base ref that was asked for and does not resolve fails the run, and at a base without the kit (a pull request into main) the directive count starts. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **Lint fixes keep the source they touch** (lint hardening 1). `ledger/no-deprecated-token --fix` replaces whole classes in place, keeping the quote character, entities, real line breaks, variants and `!`; where an escape, an entity or a template makes the edit unsafe, or the replacement would fail another ledger rule, it offers no fix and its message says why. The message names the class as written (`"hover:fill-chart-categorical-8" is deprecated; use "hover:fill-chart-categorical-7"`). `ledger/no-deprecated-name --fix` keeps an aliased or namespaced root (`DsShell.NavItem` becomes `DsShell.SideNav.Item`), never writes a prop the element already has, and keeps a value's quotes; a prop left over from a renamed part is still reported on its new name. A fix is never written where the result would not typecheck: a clsx key renamed onto a key its object already has, a string a type names, or a tag whose root is not the kit's import in scope (a local object, another package's part, a parameter that shadows it), which is reported and left. A class with a `!` at both ends keeps both, and a class whose rebuilt form another rule would report (`dark:`) is that rule's alone since 4b. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The lint reads its settings and options as given** (lint hardening 1). `settings.ledger` is checked once, whichever rules are on: an unknown key is named on stderr with the key it was likely meant to be (a short one only by case or one edit), a string `classFunctions` is one helper rather than its characters, a helper name no call can match (`styles.cn`) is said and ignored, and a value of the wrong type, `null` included, is ignored with a warning. A rule with options of its own takes `allow` beside them and keeps every constraint its schema sets; it never sees `allow`, with or without an allowance, and over its allowance a report keeps its fix, its suggestions and its words, whether it was written with a message id or in the positional form. A destructured `const` is one slot of its initialiser, so the labels beside a class (`const [label, cls] = …`) are no longer read as classes. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **A grid template with no length is structure, and stories fail on a non-token class** (lint hardening 1). `grid-cols-[auto_1fr]`, `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto]` and other templates of `auto`, `min-content`, `max-content`, `<n>fr`, `minmax(0,…)` of one of those, or a lone `subgrid` pass `no-arbitrary-value` and `no-non-token-class`; a template with a length, `repeat()` or `var()` in it is still reported. The disables in Alert, Card and Empty are gone. In the package's stories `no-non-token-class` is an error, as everywhere else, and only `src/stories` and `*.stories.tsx` files are stories: a folder named `stories` anywhere else in `src` is kit source, with every rule on. A test holds every kit source file to the package preset's rules at its severity. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The consumer smoke lints through the packed tarball** (lint hardening 1). `npm run test:consumer` checks that the tarball carries every plugin file and `src/generated/utilities.json` and nothing under `test/`, then runs ESLint through `@ledger/design-system/eslint`'s `recommended` preset: a screen with a non-token class, a margin, an arbitrary value and a local kit part gets each of those four rules, a clean screen gets nothing, and the published `package` preset carries no allowance. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **Every lint finding has a message id, a page and a note** (lint hardening 2). The rules keep their words in `meta.messages` and report by id, with what varies (the class, the part, the prop) as data, so a test or a tool reads `messageId` and `data` rather than the text. `meta.docs.url` is the rule's page, `eslint-plugin/docs/<rule>.md`, which ships with the plugin. Every rule takes a `note` option, a sentence of at most 200 characters that each of its findings ends with, for the product's own binding of the advice ("In this app: useConfirmation."); `settings.ledger.note` gives every rule one, and a note that is not a string, or is too long, is named once on stderr and ignored. Over an allowance a finding keeps its note. A report without a message id throws, and a suggestion's words take no note. Two messages change: `ledger/no-style-design-value` quotes the colour call it reports whole and as written, at any depth and with a template's `${…}` (`(hsl(calc(var(--hue) + 180) 80% 50%))`, not `(hsl(`), never a colour inside a `url()` it reads past, and `ledger/no-deprecated-name` says the same words for a member (`Shell.Sidebar`) as for a tag or an import, note included. No finding is added or removed. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **A page per lint rule, with examples the tests lint** (lint hardening 2). Each rule's page says what it reports, why, what to write instead, two to four reported and allowed examples, its fixes, its allowances and its limits. `npm run build:lint` writes the Lint rules table's rows from the pages, each with a link to its section of the new Guidance/Lint rules reference, which `llms.txt` carries after Lint rules. `test/lint-docs.test.mjs` lints every example as its path says: a reported one is reported by its rule and by no other ledger rule (with no exception since 4b gave each class one owner), an allowed one by none, and every allowed one compiles against the kit's source as the application compiles it. The Lint rules page gains "What the lint cannot see" and "When a finding looks wrong", the `use-primitives` row covers the primitives it also reads, and `AGENTS.md` sends a finding's reader to the rule's page. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **Every lint rule has cases, and its words a snapshot** (lint hardening 2). `test/lint-cases/<rule>.cases.mjs` holds each of the 27 rules' valid and invalid cases, run by `test/lint-rules.test.mjs` under espree and typescript-eslint and asserted by message id, data, location and fix output; the eight rules no test named (`button-icon-slot`, `cell-plain`, `id-not-blue`, `no-alpha-token`, `no-colgroup`, `no-dark-variant`, `no-deprecated-token`, `no-static-design-value`) are covered. `test/lint-meta.test.mjs` holds every rule to its kind, description, page, messages and a preset, and its invalid cases to reaching every message id. `test/lint-messages.test.mjs` renders every message into `test/lint-messages.snap.md` and fails when one changes (`LINT_SNAPSHOT_UPDATE=1` rewrites it), with every value a rule's data gives its words: `use-primitives`' advice for each primitive and set of kinds, `overlay-width-preset`'s presets, `no-deprecated-name`'s retired names, props and values, `no-deprecated-token`'s reasons and `no-kit-shadow`'s legacy names. Each rendering is at most 300 characters before its note, ends with a period and pairs its brackets and quotes; ten `use-primitives` renderings of three or four kinds run over until its rewrite and are listed at their length, a list that only shrinks. 17 rules are held to leading with their subject and giving no configuration advice, the other 10 until their rewrite. In this repository `npm run check:lint-changelog` fails a change to the plugin or `src/generated/utilities.json` that has no entry here, unless every commit that changed those files ends its message with the line "Lint: no behaviour change"; a change not yet committed always needs the entry, and the gate reads the whole repository from any folder. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **Two corpus ratchets, a digest of every finding, and a cheaper `no-deprecated-name`** (lint hardening 3). In this repository `npm run lint:corpus -- --check`, a step of CI's check job, lints the application's product files and this package's source, stories included, each under its own config with every allowance and inline disable taken off, and fails when a rule reports more there than `scripts/lint-corpus-baseline.json` held at the base ref; a rule new to the plugin starts its count, and a rule the base counted that the plugin no longer has fails until the baseline declares it `renamed` (the new name keeps the old count) or `removed`. The baseline also records where each scope's config runs each rule (its global ignores and every block that sets a ledger rule), and `--check` fails when a file the base had loses a rule to an off block, an ignore or a narrower glob. It also lints the class strings of shadcn's registry (from `@shadcn/lint`, its MIT notice kept, pinned to that file's sha256 by the script's tests) and a hand list of Tailwind's palette and scales, `test/fixtures/stock-classes.json`, each class on its own element in a product file. That corpus may only grow, and `--check` fails when a class the base reported now passes, unless the change names it in the baseline's `stock.admitted` with a pointer to the component library guide or What counts as structural, which must name the class, its utility or its family (`rounded-*`) in backticks; it fails, too, when more of the base's classes are reported by two or more rules. The baseline starts at 69 reports on the repository (the package's counted allowances, the avatar group's reasoned disable and the application's use-primitives and prefer-text-link allowances), 526 of 5,552 stock classes passing and 523 reported by two or more rules; `--check` fails, too, when the committed baseline is not the tree's own measurement, so a count that falls is recorded, and in a shallow clone that lacks the commit before HEAD that a push's all-zero base stands for. `--update` rewrites it and lists each rule that gained or lost a file and each stock class that changed verdict, `--rule <rule>` prints every finding of one rule, and `--digest` prints a sha256 of every finding (file, line, column, rule, message), the same on every run. `ledger/no-deprecated-name` reads a member expression's source only when the member's own name ends a renamed one, which about halves its time (about 67 to 34 ms over this package, 40 to 15 ms over the application); `test/lint-prefilter.test.mjs` lints every renamed name in every member spelling, and the rule's cases, with the check on and off and gets the same findings, and the corpus digest is the same before and after. Its map of renamed names no longer answers for the keys every object inherits, so `Kit.constructor`, `import { toString }` and `<Kit.constructor />` are no renamed part: before, the first two were reported as deprecated in favour of `undefined`, and the tag stopped the rule with a TypeError. No other finding changes. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **Lint rules know a kit part by its import** (lint hardening 4a). `eslint-plugin/identity.js` says which part a tag is, for every rule that judges one. The class-policy rules (`cell-plain`, `id-not-blue`, `button-icon-slot`, `use-primitives`, `prefer-text-link`'s TextLink and Button, and the two product layout rules) judge the kit's own part, imported from `@ledger/design-system` by name, alias (`import { Table as T }`, then `<T.Cell>`) or namespace (`<L.Table.Cell>`, three levels deep too, `<Kit.Shell.SideNav.Section>`), and never a local look-alike, another package's part, a parameter or a type-only import. The behaviour rules (`dialog-footer-order`, `link-button-navigation`, `text-link-navigation`, `no-overlay-autofocus`, `overlay-width-preset`, `no-deprecated-name`) resolve an alias or a namespace to the name it imports, the kit's or another package's (`import { DialogContent as Content } from "@/components/ui/dialog"` is DialogContent), and keep a local look-alike's name as written, since any part of that name has the same defect; a parameter, or a local that shadows an outer name, is no part. A type of a part's name (a type parameter such as `<Id extends string>`, a local type or interface) hides nothing. Their messages name the resolved part, not the alias. The `package` preset sets `settings.ledger.kit` to `"self"`, so in the kit's own source a relative import of a part (`./button`, `../components/table`, a barrel) is the part, and an import from `src/stories` or `src/lib`, or a story's own component of a part's name, is not; any other value is named once on stderr. Classes that reach a Table.Cell, an Id or a primitive through `render` (`<Slot render={<Table.Cell />} className="…">`, or a render function that spreads its props onto the part) are judged as that part's, in new words: `<Slot> renders <Table.Cell>, which is one style.`, and so is an icon inside a trigger that renders a Button (`<DialogTrigger> renders <Button>, so its icon goes in that Button's iconBefore or iconAfter`). An `a`, a `Link` or a `button` around an Id still counts by its tag, a `Button`, `TextLink`, `LinkButton` or `LinkIconButton` only when it is the kit's, and so does a trigger that renders one of them. A prop or a value named like a member of every object finds no rename: `<Switch toString="x">` was reported as deprecated in favour of `undefined`, and `<Shell.Brand toString="x">` was given a fix that wrote a native function's source into the file. `no-deprecated-name` reads prop and value renames in products (and, since 4b, in the kit's own source outside its stories). `test/lint-identity.test.mjs` tests the identity on the kit's real import shapes. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **One class reader for every rule** (lint hardening 4a). `eslint-plugin/class-sites.js` finds, once per file, every place the file hands classes to something, over the value resolver in `values.js`, and every rule that judges classes reads through it: the seven token rules, `cell-plain`, `id-not-blue`, `button-icon-slot`, `prefer-text-link`, `use-primitives`, `product-line-tabs` and `overlay-width-preset`. It follows what the file writes itself: a `const`, a `let` or `var` with each value assigned to it (and text appended after a space), a destructured slot, a parameter's defaults and the default a component gives its `className`, an entry of a map, a list, an enum or an `Object.freeze` (every entry, for a key known only at runtime), each entry of a same-file list a `.map` callback walks, a same-file function's, a `useMemo`'s, a `useCallback`'s or an immediately called function's result, `useState`'s first value, a `[…].join(" ")` and `.filter(Boolean)`, `.trim()`, `.concat()`, `Object.values()` or a spread argument, a `String.raw` template, a cva or tv recipe called where the classes land, and a readable object spread onto an element; a received `className` is the caller's, and a variable changed other than by assigning it is not read. A rule that judges one element reads only the className that lands on it: the attribute or spread written last. The class helpers are `cn`, `clsx`, `twMerge` and `classes`, and `cx`, `twJoin` and `classNames` where the file imports them (a chart's own `cx` is not one), read clsx-style, and `cva`, and `tv` where imported, read by their configs (`base`, `slots`, `variants`, `class` and `className`, never `defaultVariants`, `extend` or a compound's selectors; tv's first argument is always a config). An alias or a namespace of the kit's `cn`, clsx, classnames, tailwind-merge, class-variance-authority or tailwind-variants counts (`import { cn as merge }`, `u.cn`), and the new `settings.ledger.variantFunctions` names a product's own helpers read as `tv` is, as `settings.ledger.classFunctions` names those read as `cn` is, a name or a list of names each. A template or a concatenation that glues a value to a word (`` `bg-${tone}-500` ``, `"bg-" + tone`) is one `no-non-token-class` finding where it is written, `"bg-${tone}-500" builds a class name at runtime, which Tailwind cannot generate and the lint cannot check.`, never its fragments `bg-` and `-500`, and the whole classes beside it are still read; a hole whose values bring their own space (`` `flex${on ? " gap-100" : ""}` ``) glues nothing and is read. A `classNames` object is a slot map, read by its values at every depth, never its keys, and a `classNames` string (react-transition-group's prefix) is no class; a `class` key whose value reads as no classes (OSCAL's `"SP800-53"` and `"sp800-53a"`) is data. A module-level map or list whose strings mostly read as classes is checked where it is declared, but not its data: a bare word no rule knows (`"danger"`, `"none"`), or a key whose values across its records are mostly not classes. Each literal is checked once per rule, however many sites reach it. No finding on the repository changes (the corpus digest is the same, over 5,985 findings); the red-team matrix's escapes fall from 342 to 60, and all eleven mistakes planted in the kit's real class maps are reported, where one was before. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The class reader reaches the kit's maps, and the calls and DOM writes that carry classes** (lint hardening 4b). All 273 class strings in the kit's variant, size and tone maps (33 files) are read by each of the seven token rules, where 8 were before 4a: a mistake planted in any of them is reported, and so is one planted in each of the eleven real maps `test/lint-class-sites.test.mjs` names. More carriers are read: a key a helper's object computes from a same-file value (`cn({ [active]: on })`, and a slot map's value under a computed key); a helper held by a const (`const merge = cn`, at a class site and, module-level, as a call of its own); a same-file function's parameter, as each call gives it (Editable's `className={note("text-danger")}`, Table's `pinnedClass(pinned, edge, "z-20")`); a DOM element's classes written to `className` (`root.className = "sr-only"`, `+=`) or given to `classList.add` and `classList.toggle`; and every entry of a map with computed keys (`{ [Tone.Danger]: "…" }`) read by a key known at runtime. A module-level map is read where it is declared through an `Object.freeze`, a same-file spread, each branch of a condition, a template's own text and the values of `Object.fromEntries` or `new Map`. A bare word is no evidence that a map holds classes, so data whose dashed strings 4a read as classes is data again: a style object (`justifyContent: "space-between"`), a list of attributes (`"aria-hidden"`) or of views (`"list-detail"`), a route's search defaults (`"updated-desc"`). A name two others share is read once, so a chain of consts that each name the one below twice is read in linear time, not exponential. The token build's output under `src/generated` is not read as module-level class maps. The first rule to ask for a file's class sites reads them as ESLint's traversal meets them, and the rules after it are handed the same sites when it ends, instead of each reading every node; the reader skips the nodes that cannot be a site before remembering them, stops judging a module-level map once most of its strings cannot read as classes, and the part identity is answered once per tag for every rule that asks. Ledger rule time falls part of the way back: with each `eslint` run in its own process through the real configs (medians of seven runs), product files take 117 ms of rule time before 4a, 158 ms after it and 137 ms now, and the kit 208, 321 and 267 ms, which is 13 to 17% under the level after 4a and still 17 to 28% over the level before it, when the reader read far fewer sites. `ledger/no-deprecated-name` now reads prop and value renames in the kit's own source, on a part imported from its home module, which `npm run build:lint` records beside the part names in `eslint-plugin/components.json` (a private part of a public part's name, such as the chart's details card, keeps its own props), and still not in its stories, which show the old spelling beside the new; DataTable's pagination passed SelectTrigger the deprecated `size="sm"` and now passes `size="small"`, which the alias mapped to already, so nothing changes on screen. Reading the maps changes no finding on the repository (the corpus digest stays the same, over 5,985 findings, until the next entry's ownership). See Guidance/Lint rules reference and Patterns/DataTable.
- **One class, one finding** (lint hardening 4b). Each class the seven class rules read is reported by one of them, the one whose fix takes the whole class away or rewrites it; a rule whose fix would leave the class broken never owns it. `classify` in `eslint-plugin/classes.js` gives every class its owner, the first of: `no-dark-variant` (any `dark:` class, a margin included), `no-margin` (any other margin, at a token key, a stock key, an arbitrary value or a variable), `no-arbitrary-value`, `no-alpha-token`, `no-static-design-value`, `no-deprecated-token`, then `no-non-token-class`. So `mt-4` and `mt-[13px]` are `no-margin`'s alone, `bg-white`, `rounded` and `disabled:opacity-50` `no-static-design-value`'s, `dark:bg-white` and `dark:mt-4` `no-dark-variant`'s and `bg-brand-bold/[0.5]` `no-arbitrary-value`'s, where two or three rules reported each before. `no-dark-variant` owns a `dark:` class whatever it sets, so its words fit every one: `"dark:w-[1px]" uses the dark variant. The colour mode flips every token by itself: drop the class, and give a colour its token.`, where it asked to add a token; its messages now keep the whole message contract. `no-alpha-token` reports alpha on a token colour only (`bg-brand-bold/50`, `hover:bg-input/30`). Alpha on a Tailwind palette colour, which it called a token, is `no-non-token-class`'s, in new words: `"bg-red-500/50" is a Tailwind palette colour with alpha; neither is on the tokens.` Alpha on any other word (`bg-muted/50`) is an unknown class, whose message id is now `unknown` (it was `nonToken`), with the same words. Tailwind 4's variable shorthand has an owner: `no-arbitrary-value` reports `w-(--rail)` as `"w-(--rail)" reads a CSS variable the lint cannot compare with a token.`, and a Ledger token's own variable, typed or not (`bg-(--ds-elevation-surface)`, `bg-(color:--ds-elevation-surface)`), as an arbitrary value, with no fix yet. Grid's `grid-cols-(--ds-grid-*)` and a disclosure panel's `h-(--accordion-panel-height)` stay structural, and a typed shorthand (`bg-(image:--hero)`) is one class, no longer split at its colon. `no-deprecated-token` leaves a deprecated token under `dark:` to `no-dark-variant`, whose fix drops the class, so its `replaceAndDrop` message is gone; a replacement another rule would report is not asked for (`"<class>" is deprecated.`). Where an owner is off no other rule reports its class: a margin in `primitives/bleed.tsx`, and an arbitrary value or a variable shorthand in a story; and a line disable, an allowance or a bulk suppression of a class's owner waives the whole class at its site (`my-2` under a disable of `no-margin` is reported by no rule). A `dark:` class is `no-dark-variant`'s in all of them. A rule about a part may still quote a class its owner reports: `overlay-width-preset` names the width class on a DialogContent, SheetContent or AlertDialogContent (`max-w-[520px]`), which `no-arbitrary-value` reports too. On the repository nothing changes; on the stock corpus the 536 duplicate reports go (523 classes were reported by two or three rules, now none) and two classes move from `no-non-token-class` to `no-arbitrary-value` (`top-(--header-height)`, `w-(--radix-dropdown-menu-trigger-width)`), so the corpus digest now covers 5,449 findings. `test/lint-ownership.test.mjs` holds every stock class to the one rule `classify` names, pins the limits (with a next-line disable that applies and an allowance) and the overlay's double report, and shows that every rule reports the same alone, with the others and in the reverse order, in a product and in the kit's source. Every rule page's reported examples are reported by that rule alone. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **A part's own sizes are tokens** (lint hardening 5, decision 4). `dimension.part.*` names thirteen sizes the kit wrote as numbers, each at the value and unit it had, so nothing moves: the widest a Tooltip grows (`tooltip`, 320px), a HoverCard's and a Popover's widths (`hoverCard` 256px, `popover` 288px), the widest a CommandDialog grows (`command`, 560px), the narrowest a DropdownMenu, its submenu and a Select's list are (`menu` 128px, `submenu` 96px, `select` 9rem), the widest a Toolbar's search grows on one row (`search`, 240px), the narrowest a horizontal Stepper or Timeline is (`steps`, 420px), a Skeleton circle's size and a block's height (`skeletonCircle` 32px, `skeletonBlock` 96px), an Empty's message measure (`emptyMeasure`, 400px) and the room a KeyValue value keeps beside its label in an `auto` group (`keyValue`, 8rem). Each part reads its own through `token()`, and the group has no utility class. They are roles, not a scale: none is bound to a container size or a control height that shares its value, so the popover does not change width when `@2xs` does. Three sizes now read the token they already equalled in role: Pagination's width with its words is `dimension.container.sm`, a DataTable tree indents `space.200` a level (read from the token as the pixels `Table.Id`'s `indent` takes), and the ring an Avatar's badge and a group's circles and count wear is `border.width.selected`. See Tokens/Metrics Dimension.
- **`ledger/readable-classes`: a className on a kit part is one the lint can read** (lint hardening 5). A `className` or `*ClassName` on a kit part, where the class reader cannot read part of its value, is reported at that part, with where it comes from: another prop or parameter (one with a default too, since a caller passes any other value), a module of the product's own (held in a `const` too), another package's function the file hands values to (`join([cls, "…"], " ")`, `useContext(Ctx)`), a call the lint cannot follow, a variable written again (a list changed by `push`, `unshift`, `splice` or `fill` too), a member it cannot find, a spread it cannot read. It reads the className written on the part, in an object spread onto it, in a slot map it is given (`classNames`) and handed to a kit recipe (`buttonVariants({ className })`). What the lint reads passes: a Base UI className callback (memoised by `useCallback` too), a same-file map read by a typed prop, a forwarded `className` and the caller's own callback called with the state, the merge helpers, a `cva` or `tv` recipe, a same-file function (an argument its call leaves out is its default, or nothing), and anything imported from the kit (`buttonVariants`) or from another package by its bare name when nothing the file writes is handed to it. A class glued together at runtime, by a template, `+`, `.join("")` or `.concat()`, stays `no-non-token-class`'s. The finding offers the part's own styling props first (`Use a Button prop (variant, size, isSelected); …`, or `Use its prop (…)` when an element renders the part), which `npm run build:lint` now writes into `eslint-plugin/components.json` as `styleProps`, from each part's declared choices and `is…` flags, the ones that show a state for its data (`isActive`, `isExpanded`, `isUnread`) left out. It is an error in both presets and in stories, and lands at no finding: Primitives/Stack's blocks take `hug` in place of a width class passed as a prop, and look as they did. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **`ledger/no-style-design-value` follows the value** (lint hardening 5). A style is read wherever the file writes it: either branch and both sides of `||` and `??`, a const, a `let` never written again, a map's entry, a prop's default, an object spread into it (a received `style` is the caller's) or merged by `Object.assign`, a Base UI style callback (memoised by `useCallback` too), what a same-file function returns, each parameter read as the call gives it (`box(288)`), or `useMemo` memoises, and the objects a same-file helper merges; with a props object spread onto the element, recharts' `contentStyle`, `wrapperStyle`, `labelStyle` and `itemStyle`, and a module's const typed `CSSProperties` or `Record<…, CSSProperties>`, judged where it is written. In a value it reads the strings of a template's holes and of a concatenation, a number written straight before a length unit (`` `${W}px` ``, `W + "px"`), a named number in a sum of lengths (`offset + GAP`), a product whose every factor the file writes (`WIDTH / 16`) and a `var()`'s fallback; a factor the page computes (`depth * INDENT`) is a count, never a length. A negative number is a literal, zero in any unit never is, `flex` is a length as a string, and `containIntrinsic*Size` and `scroll(Padding|Margin)*` are lengths. A custom property's literal colour, and a literal length written whole, are reported (`style["--glow"] is a literal colour`, `style["--indent"] is a literal length`); every `*Color`, the logical border shorthands, `filter` and `textDecoration` are read for colour; colour names are the 148 CSS names as whole words (so `white-space` and `url(/icons/red.svg)` pass) and relative colour syntax built on a token passes; a part's own size, `dimension.part.*`, read anywhere but its part is reported (`style.width reads dimension.part.popover, the size Popover keeps for itself`); and a `<style>` element is reported in both presets. A colour in a module's list of colours is `no-raw-colour`'s alone. A viewport length (`100dvh`, `90vw`, `10dvh` in `min()`) is structure and passes, and the widths and heights of the Dialog and Sheet preset maps are the kit's one place for their steps. A finding says where a literal was written (`16px, from INDENT on line 12`) and, where the property's role has a scale with exactly one token of that value, names it (`On the spacing scale 16px is space.200`); a width's never, since its role is not its property's. `overlay-width-preset` reads the same objects, and in the kit a sized overlay's own `token("dimension.part.…")`, in the file that draws that part, passes, as does its own size prop falling back to it (PreviewSheet's `width ?? token(…)`). The kit's allowances fell from 39 to 34 sites (RecordBrowser's viewport widths, one each in RecordPicker and SearchDialog) and `overlay-width-preset`'s from 4 to 3 (PreviewSheet). See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **`ledger/no-raw-colour`: an SVG or chart colour is currentColor or a token** (lint hardening 5). A literal colour (a hex, a colour function, one of the 148 CSS names) in `fill`, `stroke`, `color`, `stopColor`, `floodColor` or `lightingColor` on an SVG element or a recharts part, in the `fill`, `stroke` or `color` of a recharts part's `cursor`, `activeBar`, `activeDot`, `dot`, `tick`, `label`, `labelLine`, `activeShape` or `background`, and of each record its `data` or `payload` holds (a Pie's sector paints from its record's `fill`), and a module's list of colours (reported once, where it is written) are reported, read through the value as the style rule reads it, a `var()`'s fallback and a concatenation's strings included; `currentColor`, `url(#…)`, `var()` without a fallback, relative colour syntax on a token, any call (`token()`, `chartColor()`) and a `<link>`'s or `<meta>`'s `color` pass. It is an error in both presets and in stories, and lands at no finding. `eslint-plugin/colours.js` holds the 148 names and a colour parser, from @shadcn/lint. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **Six more part sizes are tokens** (lint hardening 5, decision 4). `no-style-design-value` now reads a prop's default and what a same-file helper's call gives it, so six defaults the kit wrote as numbers are `dimension.part.*` tokens at the value and unit they had: a CodeBlock's `maxHeight` (`codeBlock`, 560px), an Item's `idWidth` (`itemId`, 72px), a DataTable column filter's and Search's `width` (`filter` 220px, `tableSearch` 200px), a PreviewSheet's `width` (`previewSheet`, 720px) and a KeyValue's label column (`keyValueLabel`, 6.5rem, which is 104px at the default text size). Each prop still takes pixels and its default is the token; nothing moves. A stacked Progress's hatch is now written on `space.025` and `border.width`, the same 2px gap and 1px line. See Tokens/Metrics Dimension, Components/Progress and Components/KeyValue Group Layouts.
- **The kit's stylesheets are held to their tokens** (lint hardening 5). `test/css-tokens.test.mjs` reads `src/styles/*.css`: no literal colour outside a `var()` reference and `theme()`, lengths only as 0, 1px, a percentage, a viewport or container length or a token, a `var()`'s fallback read as a value of its own and a number times a length as that length (`288 * 1px` is 288px), `@media` and `@container` conditions from the theme, and no `@apply`. The forced-colours outline of an Item's open row reads `border.width.selected`, at the value it had. The literals no token of their value and role holds yet (a drawer's and a toast's widths, the heading measures, the short-window height, iOS's 16px floor, a Stat grid's two hairlines, and the Shell panel header's 400px compact query, whose role token is 25rem and would scale with the text size) are counted with their reasons in `test/css-token-allow.json`, which only shrinks. See Components/Item.
- **The Lint rules page says what a style rule follows and where CSS is checked** (lint hardening 5). What the lint cannot see names how far `no-style-design-value`, `overlay-width-preset` and `no-raw-colour` follow a value in the file, that a viewport length and the Dialog and Sheet preset maps are no design value, and that the kit's own stylesheets are held to their tokens by `test/css-tokens.test.mjs` while a product's CSS is not read; a `<style>` element and an SVG or chart colour are no longer outside the rules. Since `eslint-plugin/components.json` now holds each part's styling props (in the order the source declares them, so the package build and `npm run build:lint` write the same file), `npm run build:lint` follows a change to a part's props as well as to the exports. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The lint asks Tailwind once, when the tokens are built** (lint hardening 6). `npm run build:tokens` also runs `build/lint-data.mjs`, which loads Tailwind 4.3.3 once on the kit's own entry and writes two files beside the allowlist. `src/generated/lint.json` holds the name of every `@utility`, the utilities a leading minus negates, the variant grammar with the breakpoints and container sizes, the ARIA attribute names (aria-query's, and the three ARIA 1.3 adds that it lacks), the Tailwind version and the hash of each file it was built from. `src/generated/lint-values.json` holds each `@utility`'s file, line and properties, each token's value (px, ms, type metrics, OKLab per mode with alpha composited over the surface), the token behind each token class and Tailwind's stock scales. The plugin reads them through `eslint-plugin/data.js`, both from `src/generated` or else both from `dist/generated`: the facts on the first file that needs them, the values only when a finding needs one, and never Tailwind itself. Stale data is one finding at line 1 of each file, from whichever of `no-arbitrary-value`, `no-unknown-variant` and `no-non-token-class` runs first, naming what changed: in the kit's checkout, a file the data was built from (the allowlist, the token docs, a stylesheet) changed since the build; anywhere, a `lint-values.json` from another build than `lint.json`. `no-arbitrary-value` writes no token class until the data is fresh, and no allowance counts the finding; a product's installed package, which ships without the token docs, compares nothing. A missing or unreadable file is an error that names it. `ledger.css` keeps both files out of Tailwind's scan, since they spell classes no element renders. No rule's verdict changes. `@tailwindcss/node` and `aria-query`, already installed, are declared as the package's devDependencies. See Guidance/Lint rules, What the lint knows of Tailwind.
- **Tailwind's 2xl breakpoint is gone** (lint hardening 6, decision 5). The generated `reset.css` clears `--breakpoint-*` before the kit's breakpoints are set, so the breakpoints are the kit's seven and `2xl:` generates nothing; `xl` stays, the alias of `panel` behind Grid's `xl` column count. Nothing in the kit or the application used `2xl:`, and nothing on screen moves. See Tokens/Metrics, Breakpoints.
- **The allowlist admits only what Tailwind generates** (lint hardening 6). The kit's own `@utility` names come from its CSS, which the token build reads into `lint.json`: a utility the CSS gains passes `no-non-token-class` with no edit to the lint, and one it loses fails. The structural patterns are tightened so every spelling they admit generates CSS in Tailwind 4.3.3, and 412 that generated nothing leave, none of them used in the kit or the application: a minus on padding, gap or a size (`-p-200`), a minus before `auto` (`-mt-auto`), an alignment value its property does not take (`items-between`), `grid-cols-0`, a number with a leading zero or more than 15 digits (`order-01`), `size-screen`, `float-both`, `inert`, and any `recharts-*` name but the tick class the chart reads. The alignment values Tailwind added pass (`items-center-safe`, `items-baseline-last`, `justify-end-safe`). A minus on a padding, gap or size, which Tailwind does not negate, has its own message, which names the property and, for padding and gap, Bleed: `"-p-200" generates no CSS: padding cannot be negative. Drop the minus, or pull the content outward with Bleed.` `test/lint-tailwind.test.mjs` asks Tailwind about every spelling the lint admits. In the stock corpus `size-screen` is now reported. See Guidance/Lint rules, What counts as structural.
- **`ledger/no-arbitrary-value` fixes a token's variable to its token class** (lint hardening 6). `bg-(--ds-elevation-surface)`, `bg-[var(--ds-elevation-surface)]` and their typed forms become `bg-surface`, `p-[var(--ds-space-200)]` becomes `p-200` and `rounded-t-(--ds-radius-medium)` becomes `rounded-t-medium`: the token build pairs each token's variable with the token class Tailwind generates the same CSS for (`varToClass` in `lint-values.json`), and `--fix` writes it in the string's own source, with the class's variants and `!`. Where a template, an escape or an entity rules out the edit, the message names the class and says why. Where no token class declares the same, or the one that does is deprecated, the finding is the arbitrary value it was. See Guidance/Lint rules reference, ledger/no-arbitrary-value.
- **`ledger/no-unknown-variant`: every variant is one Tailwind generates** (lint hardening 6). A class under a variant Tailwind does not generate adds no CSS, and nothing says so: `hovr:`, `grp-hover:`, `selected:`, `group-not-hover:` (a group cannot wrap `not-hover`, which is a media query as well as a selector), a `/name` on a variant that takes none (`hover/row:`, whose message names `hover` and `group-hover/row`), a breakpoint the kit does not have (`tablet:`, `2xl:`) and a container size that is none (`@8xl:`). An `aria-` variant whose name is no ARIA attribute (`aria-expaned:`), and a selector or feature query whose value is a CSS variable (`nth-(--n):`, `supports-(--x):`), generate CSS no element matches. The rule checks every variant against the grammar in `lint.json`, compounds through to the variant they wrap; the token build also records there the variants a negation splits in two (`hover`). It is an error in both presets and in stories. Its message names the variant's one spelling when there is one, which an editor suggestion writes, never `--fix`, when the respelled class passes every class rule; a breakpoint or a container size is never respelled, nor a variant another rule forbids (`dark`), and a word that reads as a size (a digit, a t-shirt size, a device, a name near a breakpoint's) gets a message that lists the container sizes first, then the window breakpoints, from the data; any other word is a state. It owns a class after `no-dark-variant` and `no-margin` and before every rule that judges the base. It lands at 0 findings in the kit, its stories and the application; in the stock corpus `2xl:text-5xl` is now its. A variant a product declares in its own CSS (`@custom-variant`) is not in the grammar, which is read from the kit's CSS: the new `settings.ledger.customVariants` names it, and the rule then knows it, so the rule stays on and the other class rules judge what the class holds. `test/lint-variants.test.mjs` holds the grammar to Tailwind over every variant the sources and the stock corpus write and a fuzz corpus of misspellings and compounds three deep. See Guidance/Lint rules reference, ledger/no-unknown-variant.
- **`ledger/no-non-token-class` names the Ledger class for a shadcn theme name** (lint hardening 7). `text-muted-foreground`, `bg-background`, `bg-primary`, `ring-ring` and 35 more of shadcn's colour names generate no CSS in Ledger; each is now reported as what it is in shadcn and the Ledger class for the same job (`"text-muted-foreground" is shadcn's secondary text colour, which generates no CSS in Ledger. Use text-subtle.`), or the two and when each fits (`bg-muted`: `bg-neutral` for a neutral fill, `bg-surface-sunken` for a recessed region), and `bg-accent` adds that Ledger's `accent-*` colours are hues with no meaning. The class is an editor suggestion under the same variants and `!`, never a `--fix`; with alpha (`bg-muted/50`) the message names the colour and leaves the shade to a state token, and nothing is suggested. The table is `build/vocabulary-aliases.json`, outside the tokens: `npm run build:tokens` checks it (each name generates no CSS and is no Ledger class, each class is one `utilities.json` lists, not deprecated and not for one purpose only) and writes it into `src/generated/lint-values.json`, with the names in `lint.json`, and `npm run build:lint` writes it onto the Coming from shadcn page between markers, where a test holds it. `border-input` and `bg-input` are Ledger's own tokens too, which the page no longer says are missing. Which classes are reported is unchanged. See Guidance/Coming from shadcn.
- **A finding names the Ledger token nearest the value, from its role's family** (lint hardening 7, decision 8). `eslint-plugin/nearest.js` reads the token values in `lint-values.json` the first time a finding needs one, and names the token of the value's role: a space for padding, gap, inset and translate, a type style for a text size, then a weight, a radius, a border width, a duration, the two opacities and a drop shadow; never a container or breakpoint threshold, a layout width or a part's own size, so a width or a height is told it is a layout part's, a preset's or computed. `no-non-token-class` names a stock class's nearest token (`"p-4" is Tailwind's spacing step 4 (16px), which Ledger's tokens replace. 16px is p-200 (space.200).`), respells a misspelt token class when one is nearest (`"bg-surfce" is "bg-surface" misspelt`), and names the keys either side of a space key that is no key (`p-210`) and the range of a series a class steps past (`fill-chart-categorical-9`); `no-arbitrary-value` names the nearest token of an arbitrary length, colour, duration or opacity; `no-static-design-value` names the nearest radius, duration and border width, where it named fixed ones (its `duration-200` hint was wrong: the nearest are `duration-moderate` and `duration-medium`); and `no-style-design-value` names the two nearest on its property's scale, a font size's type class and a literal colour's roles, where it named a token only for an exact value. A colour always gets the roles its hue plays, each with its purpose (`Red is bg-danger-bold (…) or bg-accent-red-bolder (…)`), in the state its variant asks for; a colour and its `dark:` twin on the same site are ranked together, in both modes. One class is an editor suggestion only when it has exactly the value (`p-4` offers `p-200`, `border-t-[1px]` `border-t`), or a colour pair matches one token within ΔE 0.02 in both modes; it passes every rule, and never runs under `--fix`. `darkPairAdvice` gives `no-dark-variant` the same ranking. While the lint data is stale, nothing is named. Which classes are reported is unchanged. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **Every lint message says what broke, why, and what to write** (lint hardening 7). The last nine rules whose words did not keep the message contract now lead with their subject as written and name the fix: `button-icon-slot` (`<PlusIcon> is sized by hand ("size-icon-small") as a Button's child, … Pass it bare: iconBefore={<PlusIcon />} or iconAfter.`), `dialog-footer-order` (the late cancel, the footer and what it dismisses), `id-not-blue`, `no-disabled-while-loading` (the element and the flag it shares, and a props object's flag), `no-native-confirm` (the call, `window.confirm()`), `no-overlay-autofocus` (`<Input autoFocus> is inside DialogContent, …`), `prefer-text-link`, `product-line-tabs` and `product-responsive-table` (how the prop is written: `takes the default variant`, `sets responsive={false}`, `leaves responsive to a prop spread`). Every rule is now held to the contract by `test/lint-messages.test.mjs`, and every rendering is fitted to 300 characters, the longest names included: a list of pending flags past what fits names the first and how many more, and a component name too long for the limit its last part. Which code is reported is unchanged. See Guidance/Lint rules reference.
- **Lint advice names the prop and its token value** (lint hardening 7). `use-primitives` names the part a plain element's classes make, one sentence with its props and their values: a flex row is an Inline, a flex column (`flex-col`) a Stack, a grid a Grid, padding a Box (`<div> carries layout classes (flex, gap-100). Use <Inline space="space.100">.`, `Use <Stack space="space.200"> inside <Box padding="space.300">.`); on a primitive, the display it has already goes, its props take the values (`templateColumns={{ md: "repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr))" }}`) and padding goes on a Box around it. `no-margin` names the parent's space at the margin's step, a Stack's for the block axis and an Inline's for the inline axis (`a Stack with space="space.200"`), and a negative margin `<Bleed inline="space.100">`. A stock step or an arbitrary length takes the step it equals, or the one nearest it, which the finding says. `no-style-design-value` names a padding's or a gap's prop and class when the literal is a space step exactly (`Use padding="space.200" on a Box, or p-200`, message id `spaceLength`; the prop alone on a plain layout element or a primitive in a product, and the class first in the kit's own source). `overlay-width-preset` names the step of each part's own map nearest the width written (`DialogContent sets maxWidth in style (480px). Use width="medium" (520px), the nearest step; …`), from the Dialog's and the Sheet's width maps and AlertDialog's size classes, which `npm run build:lint` reads from their source into `eslint-plugin/components.json`'s `presets`, with no new tokens; `DrawerContent` is told it takes no width. None is an editor suggestion or a fix. See Guidance/Lint rules reference, ledger/use-primitives.
- **`no-alpha-token` names the state for its alpha, and `no-dark-variant` reads the light twin** (lint hardening 7). An alpha on a token names that token's own states from the lint data, picked by the alpha: at `/30` or below its tints (the subtle, subtler or subtlest form nearest the colour the alpha makes over the page), at `/60` or above its hovered and pressed states, in between one of each, and the state a `hover:` or `active:` variant asks for when the token has it (`"hover:bg-neutral/50" dims bg-neutral with alpha, … That state is its own token: hover:bg-neutral-hovered.`). A `dark:` colour is read with its light twin on the same site (nearest-token pairing): beside a token it only goes (`"dark:bg-gray-900" sets a dark value by hand beside "bg-surface", a token that already changes with the mode.`), a token alone under `dark:` is written without it, and a pair that is no token is ranked in both modes and named by the roles its hue plays, a single token only when it matches in both modes (decision 8). A class that sets no colour keeps the rule's words. Which classes are reported is unchanged. See Guidance/Lint rules reference, ledger/no-dark-variant.
- **The Lint rules page says what a finding names** (lint hardening 7). A new section, What a finding names, sets out the message contract every rule now keeps (the subject as written, what it is, why, and the token, prop value or part to write, never the configuration), the nearest token taken only from the family of the property's role (a width or a height names none), colours by the roles their hue plays with one token only within ΔE 0.02 in both modes (decision 8), shadcn's theme names, the part and prop advice, and which findings carry an editor suggestion and which a fix: `--fix` writes only the one-to-one fixes and never runs a suggestion. One owner per class names `no-non-token-class`'s new causes, What the lint knows of Tailwind lists the theme names and the palette the advice ranks against, and What the lint cannot see gains what a value is for. `test/lint-fix-safety.test.mjs` applies every suggestion of the 39 theme names, bare and under `hover:!`, and holds each to the closure check and to no `--fix`. See Guidance/Lint rules, What a finding names.
- **Lint advice that names the fix, reviewed** (lint hardening 7). A suggestion, and the class a message names, now holds where it is written: on an element `use-primitives` reads, in a product, a padding or a gap is named as its primitive's prop (`16px is space.200: padding="space.200" on a Box.`) with no class suggestion, which that rule would report in turn; `space-x` and `space-y` name the parent's space. Decision 8 is kept whole: one token only for a colour one role holds within ΔE 0.02 in both modes, both when two roles hold it, and a grey pair only where a token twin names its role (`bg-white dark:bg-zinc-950` names `bg-surface-sunken` or `bg-surface`, and offers neither). A pale tint is its hue's by its lightness too (`bg-[#fef2f2]` is red, as `bg-red-50` is), a palette colour with alpha names its hue's roles as it shows over each mode's page, and `data-[disabled]:` is a disabled state. A respelling stays within the class's utility, never swaps one emphasis of a token for another and never touches a class that ends in a Tailwind value (`min-h-dvw` is Tailwind's, and reported as unknown). Zero is `space.0`, 9999px `rounded-full` and a radius from 32px a pill's, 1px `h-px` or `w-px`, a size's example its own dimension, a type step that sets a weight is named with it and never picked, an outline's width the border widths, `ring-[3px]` the focus outline, `font-[500]` `font-medium`, `delay-[150ms]` a duration token, `ease-in-out` `ease-standard`, and a negative offset in style keeps its sign. `no-dark-variant` has `literalTwin` for a token under `dark:` beside a literal colour, and every pair falls back to its roles alone under a long chain; `no-margin` reads a bracketed negative as Bleed and stops at Bleed's widest step, `space.400`; `use-primitives` keys `templateColumns` only by `base`, `sm`, `md`, `lg` and `xl`, gives a Stack no column gap, and says what a lone `gap-px` needs; `no-alpha-token` names only a tint quieter than the token, and never the other band's states. The theme names gain `bg-sidebar-border` and `fill-foreground`, and `states`: `bg-selected` or `text-selected` on a selected, checked or pressed item and `text-subtlest` for a placeholder. "An orange" replaces "A orange" in two accent descriptions. Which classes are reported is unchanged. See Guidance/Lint rules, What a finding names.
- **A Scroller keeps the focused item in view when it changes size** (DSC-12 follow-up). Focus revealed on arrival stayed put when the strip narrowed under it, so a tab reached with End during a resize or a side nav folding to its rail ended past the edge; on a change of size the focused item is revealed again, after the current one. See Components/Tabs Late counts on a phone.
- **Calendar heads a column with the narrow weekday where the short one is a word** (coverage-gaps G1-3 follow-up). The base `overflow-wrap` rule broke Arabic's short names ("الأربعاء") inside a 32px day column; a locale whose short names run past four characters now shows the narrow form, and every header keeps the full name as its label. See Components/Calendar Localized.
- **`ledger/no-deprecated-name` renames props and values, not only parts** (audit API-6, API-13, API-16, CNT-16, CTL-17, TOO-12, GATES). On a part imported from the kit, `--fix` writes `size="small"` and `"medium"` for Switch's, SelectTrigger's and Card's `sm` and `default`, `variant="danger"` for DropdownMenuItem's `destructive`, and `aria-labelledby`, `centerLabel`, `nameKey`, `state` and `isExpanded` for Item.Group's `labelledBy`, Chart.Donut's `label`, Chart.Scatter's `name`, Chart.Frame's `status` and Tree.Item's `expanded`; it reports Chart.Area's `baseline`, imports of `controlBase`, `controlHeight` and the chart's `formatNumber`, and imports of the removed IndexPage, ShowPage, RecordHeader, PreviewRail, PreviewSplit, Panel and Block with the part that replaces each. A local component that shares a kit name is left alone. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **`ledger/prefer-text-link` reports a new tab that is not announced** (audit CNT-18, GAP-13, GATES). `target="_blank"` on a raw `a`, `Link` or `NavLink`, or on a TextLink (or its render element) without `newTab`, is reported: TextLink `newTab` sets `rel` and says "(opens in a new tab)". A link a part holds in a prop, such as PreviewNavigation's `openLink`, is that part's to announce. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **`ledger/use-primitives` reads the primitives' own classes** (audit PRM-7, TOO-13, GATES). Padding, gap, display and grid-template classes on Box, Stack, Inline, Flex and Grid are reported with what the part takes instead: Box's padding props, Stack's and Inline's `space`, Flex's and Grid's `gap`, Grid's `templateColumns`, and, since only Box has padding, a Box around a padded Stack, Inline, Flex or Grid. Layout classes on `p`, `label` and `h1` to `h6` are reported as on a `div`. A container-query class (`@4xl:grid-cols-3`), responsive visibility (`hidden @3xl:flex`), a breakpoint's padding or gap (`md:px-300`, which the one-token props cannot say) and `gap-px` pass. The rule stays a warning in `recommended`. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The structural list takes `wrap-*` and the chart library's classes** (audit G1-3, PGL-18, CHF-17, GATES). `wrap-normal`, `wrap-break-word` and `wrap-anywhere` (overflow-wrap) and a `recharts-*` class the chart library reads are structural; `grid-cols-main-rail`, which nothing defines, is no longer accepted. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **The package is linted with the recommended JavaScript, TypeScript and hooks rules** (audit TOO-6, GATES). `eslint.config.js` adds `@eslint/js` and typescript-eslint recommended and the rules of hooks (exhaustive-deps a warning for now); a leading underscore marks what a destructure leaves out, and a story's `render` counts as a component. The sites that predated them are counted per file in `eslint-suppressions.json`, which ESLint applies itself and which may only shrink (`npm run lint -- --prune-suppressions` lowers a count once a site is fixed).
- **A story that still moves under reduced motion fails** (audit G8-8, GATES). storybook-dark, which asks for reduced motion, runs a motion gate: an element transitioning transform, translate, rotate, scale, width, height, an inset or `all` for longer than 0.01 ms, a movement animation still running for longer than 10 ms, or a moving transition started during the render or play (dnd-kit's inline transforms) fails the story. Every story passes today, so `test/gates-allow.json` lists none. See Guidance/Testing and review.
- **ds:check reaches ActionBar and WorkPane, and checks the props tables** (audit API-8, API-10, TLB-16, GAP-10, TOOLSDOCS). The retired-export exemption is gone: a stories file's meta `component` counts as rendered when a story renders from args, so ActionBar's and WorkPane's stories cover them. `npm run ds:check` now fails when a compound's member is a function its module does not export (docgen documents a module's exports, so the member would get no props table) and when a family page renders no `<ArgTypes>` for its stories' component; it reports, without failing yet, the exported families with no page showing generated props (the DataTable family's), and how many catalog parts the application imports (`-- --usage` lists the rest). No story changes.
- **The Storybook loads its typefaces as a product does** (audit TOK-6, TOOLSDOCS). `preview-head.html` no longer links Google Fonts: Geist comes from the package's `fonts.css` alone, which `storybook.css` imports, so every story renders the faces a consumer gets. Code text falls back to JetBrains Mono or the system monospace until the package ships Geist Mono. See Tokens/Typography.
- **llms.txt carries the props tables** (audit TOO-2, TOOLSDOCS). `npm run build:llms` runs the Storybook's own docgen with its options (`.storybook/docgen.mjs`, which `main.ts` reads too) and writes each `<ArgTypes of={Part} />` block as a table of the part's props, the Base UI props it inherits included, with type, default and description, where it used to write a pointer. A block whose part has no props of its own keeps the pointer. See Guidance/Agents.
- **Guidance follows the kit it describes** (audit GAP-15, REC-14, NAV-15, PGL-13, PRV-8, REC-13, DOC-10, DOC-17, BTN-13, CNT-17, G1-13, G3-6, D5, D6, D12, D13, TOOLSDOCS). Choosing a part names the Gates row by the lists it replaces, adds rows for WorkPane, reordering, CSV export, virtualizing, which scroller, and a Not in the kit list. Recipes adds a dashboard (linked Stat.Tiles, top-N sections with View all, a Timeline feed) and working through a list with WorkPane, and the preview recipe walks every page, names the record it announces, and says what Close, Escape and Back do. Which token and Token grammar match the type ramp and the eight palette ramps; Testing and review and Writing stories describe the gate projects and the console-warning check. The component library guide gains the size scale, the heading-level policy, a Long content section, the focus-ring rule for clipping parts and the Shell's 1024 to 1279px panel placement. Guidance sorts in reading order. See Guidance/Choosing a part and Guidance/Recipes.
- **The Lint rules page lists every rule, and a test holds it to the plugin** (audit TOO-13, DOC-11, TOOLSDOCS). The six rules the gates batch added (`no-overlay-autofocus`, `no-disabled-while-loading`, `overlay-width-preset`, `no-plain-alert-role`, `link-button-navigation`, `no-style-design-value`) have rows with their preset settings, the stories' exemptions and the package's counted allowances in `test/lint-allow.json`. `test/lint-docs.test.mjs` fails when a rule has no row, a row names no rule, or a preset column disagrees with the preset. See Guidance/Lint rules.
- **Breaking: the source is behind the named `@ledger/source` condition** (audit TOO-11, TOOLSDOCS). `exports` maps `@ledger/source` to `src/index.ts` and `src/lib/cn.ts`, and every other condition, the generic `development` included, to `dist/`, so a webpack or Next.js dev server is handed JavaScript rather than TypeScript it would not compile. A workspace that compiled the source under `development` adds `@ledger/source` to its bundler's dev conditions and TypeScript's `customConditions`, as this repository's app does in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`. `npm run test:consumer` checks that the tarball resolves dist under `development` and source under `@ledger/source`. Guidance/Getting started, Install, says how to opt in.
- **A className overrides the kit's widths and side radii** (audit TOO-5, TOOLSDOCS). The generated tailwind-merge config (`src/generated/merge-config.ts`) groups the `w-layout-*`, `min-w-control-*` and `max-w-layout-measure` utilities with Tailwind's own width classes and extends the radius and spacing scales, so `cn("w-layout-rail", "w-full")` keeps `w-full`, `min-w-0` replaces `min-w-control-medium`, and `rounded-t-none` replaces `rounded-t-medium`, where both used to stay and the kit's token class won. `test/merge-config.test.mjs` runs every generated utility through `cn`. No story changes; the test shows it.
- **Props tables list the Base UI props a part inherits** (audit TOO-2, DOC-1, TOOLSDOCS). The Storybook's docgen keeps every prop declared by `@base-ui/react` (a Dialog's `open` and `onOpenChange`, a Switch's `checked` and `onCheckedChange`, a Select's `value`, `multiple` and `items`) and still leaves out the DOM's own attributes from `@types/react`, so the ArgTypes tables and MCP `docs-show` list a part's controlled state, form and callback props. Switch lists 17 props where it listed 4, Select 23 where it listed none. The running Storybook shows them after a restart. See Components/Switch and Components/Dialog, Props.
- **A chart that chooses is one tab stop and says each step; one that chooses nothing is an image** (audit CHF-1, CHX-1, CHF-10, CHX-3, D9, CHARTA). A Bar, Line or Area with `onSelect` or `details` is `role="application"` with the role description "chart" (locale `chartRole`), named by the Frame's title (`aria-labelledby`) or its `label`, and described by its keys (`chartKeysColumns`, `chartKeysRows`). Left and Right walk the categories; on a horizontal bar chart Down and Right go to the next and Up and Left to the one before, where recharts had Right do nothing and Left go down; Home and End go to either end; Enter and Space choose the category the keys are on, even with a pointer resting on another, and a plot the keyboard comes back to shows its tooltip where the keys left it. Each step writes the category and every visible value, then a stack's total, into one persistent polite live region (`chartPoint`, `chartSeriesValue`, `chartTotalValue`). A named plot that chooses nothing is `role="img"` with no tab stop. A plot that chooses with no name is named "Chart" (`chartUnnamed`) and warns once in development. The svg carries `data-chart-surface`, which the focus ring, the focus return and the PNG read instead of recharts' class names. See Patterns/Chart/Bar Horizontal, Single and Patterns/Chart/Overview Details, Framed.
- **Every category keeps a label** (audit CHX-2, CHF-14, CHX-18, CHARTA). Tick labels carry recharts' tick class and a `tickFormatter` of the text they print, so recharts thins them by the drawn text in the tick's font, not by epoch milliseconds or the raw value. A column chart prints every category: a name that does not fit its band takes two lines, and a line past the band is cut with an ellipsis measured to the band, the whole name in the tick's title. A horizontal chart cuts its names to the measured column, not 18 characters. A line's categories all print when they fit their slots; a time axis keeps its first and last ticks. See Patterns/Chart/Bar Every Category and Patterns/Chart/Line Dates.
- **A time axis reads in the reader's zone and says the year** (audit CHF-7, CHX-4, CHARTA). Ticks fall on the starts of hours, days, months and years in the provider's `timeZone`, so a tick labelled "Feb" is the first of February there, west of UTC too; data of calendar days ("2026-09-04") reads in UTC, the same day everywhere. Months step by 1, 2, 3, 6 or 12 aligned to January, years by round steps, at most eight ticks; the first tick and every tick that starts a new year carry the year in full ("Apr 2026", which "Apr 26" would not say). See Patterns/Chart/Line Zones and Dates.
- **A value axis steps in round numbers through zero** (audit CHX-7, CHARTA). With both ends of `domain` pinned, and on any axis that crosses zero, the kit sets round ticks (1, 2, 2.5 or 5 times a power of ten; whole steps for whole data) that include zero, widening an unpinned axis to its round ends, and every such tick prints. The Negatives story's variance reads "−5 days … +10 days" with a true minus sign. See Patterns/Chart/Bar Negatives.
- **A stack totals its parts, at its end too** (audit CHX-8, CHARTA; missing.md "Total at the end of a stacked bar"). The tooltip's total sums the stacked series only, never the `line` or the target; a stack with parts below zero stacks each side from zero (`stackOffset="sign"`); `labels="end"` on a stack prints each stack's total at its end. A column chart's end labels print only when every one fits its bar's share of the category. "Total" is the locale's `chartTotal`. See Patterns/Chart/Bar Bar Matrix.
- **End labels say whose they are** (audit CHX-5, CHX-13, CHARTA). On a stacked Area each end label sits on its band's top, at the running total, and prints the band's own value. A line's end label pushed off its line's end leads with a stroke of the series' colour. The right margin holds the widest end label rather than a fixed 44px. See Patterns/Chart/Area Area Matrix and Patterns/Chart/Line Burndown.
- **A hidden series keeps the rest in place** (audit CHX-10, CHX-11, CHF-20, CHARTA). The value axis keeps its scale when the legend hides a series (`includeHidden`); a stack takes round steps over every part, hidden ones included, and a hidden part leaves the stack, which closes up over zero rather than floating over a gap; every series takes its colour from its place among all of them, hidden ones included, in the plot, the tooltip and the card; while the table shows, a hidden series leaves its columns too, and the CSV keeps every one. Each row of the table twin is headed by its category (`th scope="row"`). See Patterns/Chart/Line Lines, Patterns/Chart/Bar Stacked and Patterns/Chart/Overview Follows Its Part.
- **A chart's empty and failed states are the kit's** (audit CHF-11, CHARTA). Empty is a compact Empty at the plot's height with its `statusText` and a new `statusAction`; error is a danger Alert, `role="alert"`, with Try again from the new `onRetry` beside its message, as a failed DataTable has. A Frame `ready` whose `data` (or a part's records) holds nothing says it is empty instead of drawing bare axes; the part stays mounted, drawing nothing, so the Frame hears when records arrive. See Patterns/Chart/Overview States.
- **The CSV opens in a spreadsheet, and the PNG keeps its name and key** (audit CHF-8, CHF-9, CHARTA). The CSV starts with a byte order mark, puts CRLF between lines, quotes a return, writes a text cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a return as text, and names its file with letters in any script; a Date prints as ISO 8601. The PNG draws the title, the line and the legend above the plot in the page's fonts, copies the patterns its fills point at, and paints on the surface under the chart. Download PNG is disabled, with its reason, where the part draws no image (`chartNoImage`) and while the table shows (`chartImageInTable`); a PNG that cannot be drawn says so in an alert under the header (`chartImageFailed`). Downloads go through the kit's `downloadText` and `downloadBlob`. See Patterns/Chart/Overview Downloads and Textured.
- **The Frame's title is in the outline, and its header folds cleanly** (audit CHF-18, CHF-19, CHARTA). Inside a HeadingLevelProvider or a titled Section the title is a heading at that level; `titleLevel` sets one outright. The figure is described by its line and its `summary`, and the summary is no longer read again in the caption. In a Frame narrower than 32rem the tools stay on the title's row and the legend and actions take the next row from the start. The expanded Dialog is DialogContent's `xlarge` step, and the Download menu sizes to its items. See Patterns/Chart/Overview Headings, Narrow and Framed.
- **Sparkline: its own name for the value, and a scale that shows a trend on a high base** (audit CHX-12, STA-16, CHARTA). `seriesLabel` (else `label`) names the value in the tooltip, which prints the category only when `x` is given; `baseline` is `auto` by default for a line or an area, so 96% to 99% no longer lies flat along the top, and `zero` for bars. A named sparkline is an image with that name and never a tab stop. The In tiles story uses Stat.Tile's `trend`. See Patterns/Chart/Sparkline Baseline, In Tiles and Narrow.
- **The plots take native props and a ref** (audit CHX-15, CHARTA). Chart.Bar, Line, Area and Sparkline pass an `id`, `data-*` and handlers to their box and forward a ref to it; `aria-describedby` describes the plot's svg, beside its keys. See Patterns/Chart/Bar Horizontal.
- **Deprecated: Chart.Area's `baseline`** (audit CHX-14, CHARTA). An area's wash measures from zero, so its value axis always starts there and the prop is ignored; a cropped trend is a Line with `baseline="auto"`. See Patterns/Chart/Area Area Matrix.
- **A chart's key keeps its colours in forced colours** (audit CHF-6, CHX-6, CHARTA). The legend's, the tooltip's and the card's swatches carry `data-slot="chart-swatch"`, which keeps its colour (`forced-color-adjust: none`) with a CanvasText outline, as the svg marks keep theirs. See Patterns/Chart/Overview Framed and Textured in the forced-colours project.
- **Chart internals, one file per concern** (audit CHF-13, CHF-17, CHO-1, G8-2, FLT-2, MNU-8, G6-1, CHF-12, CHARTA). `_shared.tsx` is an index of fourteen internal modules; the pure ones (`_values`, `_time`, `_csv`, `_labels`) run under `node --test` in test/chart.test.mjs, covering the time ticks in UTC and New York, the CSV's cells, the value ticks, the label row and the category fit. `useReducedMotion` moves to `src/lib/use-reduced-motion.ts` for any part to share. A Plot given `textures` draws its patterns beside the chart, so every fill that points at them paints whatever the part draws. The details card is the kit's PopoverContent with `anchor`, and the chart's surface strokes use the current surface, so a chart in a Card or a Dialog is ringed in that surface. The unused `useInFrame`, `formatCategory` and Plot's `busy` are gone, and the public `formatNumber` is deprecated in favour of `useLedgerLocale().formatNumber`. See test/chart.test.mjs and Patterns/Chart/Overview Textured.
- **A narrow WorkPane is a drill-in** (audit REC-1, PA1-1, D5, RECPATTERNS). Below 768px of pane the list shows alone; choosing a row puts its detail in the list's place with a Back button above it and focus on the detail's first heading, and Back returns focus to the row, scrolled into view. Both sides stay mounted, so a draft and the search survive Back. The pane keeps which side shows (`defaultView`, or `view` with `onViewChange`), `backLabel` names Back ("Back to list" from the locale's `workPaneBack`), and a row's `onSelect` may return a promise that opens the detail once it settles, unless it resolves `false`. `detail` is optional: leave it undefined while nothing is chosen and `empty` shows. When the pane narrows under the reader (a resize, a zoom), it keeps the side that holds focus on screen, and when the detail goes while it holds focus, focus returns to the list. See Patterns/WorkPane WorkPane, Drill-in, long list and Guarded choice.
- **A WorkPane list is one Tab stop** (audit REC-2, CNT-3, RECPATTERNS). The row last focused, else the open row, else the first, is the list's stop; Up, Down, Home, End and type-ahead move between rows, and the open row carries `aria-current`. In forced colours the open row is outlined in Highlight. See Patterns/WorkPane One Tab stop.
- **WorkPane names its list and takes its tools** (audit REC-10, REC-20, RECPATTERNS). `listLabel` names both the landmark and the list (the locale's `workPaneList`, "List", when there is none); `listToolbar` holds the search, a filter and the count under it; `listEmpty` is what the list shows with no rows. See Patterns/WorkPane Nothing chosen and no matches and Dont.
- **A focused WorkPane row lands below the sticky label** (audit G5-4, RECPATTERNS). The pane measures the label and toolbar that stay put over the rows, with its scroller's padding, and each row's control scrolls in below them, in the pane's own scroller or a dialog's body. See Patterns/WorkPane In a dialog.
- **WorkPane.Row is named by its id and title** (audit REC-11, STS-6, RECPATTERNS). The name reads "AC-2, Account management" (the locale's `workPaneRowName`, `{id}, {title}`), the meta is its description, and a `tone` other than `neutral` needs the `meta` that says it in words. `WorkPaneRow` and `WorkPaneView` are exported by name. See Patterns/WorkPane WorkPane.
- **Gates takes a name and speaks through the locale** (audit REC-21, REC-20, RECPATTERNS). `aria-labelledby` and `aria-label` name the list, which keeps `role="list"` in WebKit; "Met:" and "Not met:" come from the locale's `gateMet` and `gateNotMet` (`Met: {label}`), and the page recommends actions named for their gate ("Add success criterion"). `GateItem` is exported by name. See Patterns/Gates In Rail and Gates.
- **TaskRow's checkbox keeps one name** (audit REC-12, CTL-13, REC-20, REC-14, RECPATTERNS). The checkbox is named for the task, the title by default, and its checked state says whether it is done; the name no longer flips between "Complete:" and "Reopen:". A completed row without a checkbox is read with the locale's `taskRowCompleted` (`{title}, completed`); a `completedLabel` without `{title}` is still said after the title. See Patterns/TaskRow Completion and Task Row Matrix.
- **Glance and Gates have plays** (audit TOO-17, RECPATTERNS). Glance's opens its card from the keyboard and checks the reading order and the four-fact limit; Gates' check the spoken state, the list's name and the named actions. See Patterns/Glance Glance and Patterns/Gates Gates.
- **A submit from outside a stacked WorkPane shows the detail it submits** (audit REC-1, D5, RECPATTERNS review). When a dialog's primary submits the detail's form while the list shows, the detail comes back and focus stays for the form, which takes it to the field that needs fixing or leaves it on the primary; before, the field was hidden and could not take focus. See Patterns/WorkPane In a dialog.

- **RecordBrowser names records, never their keys** (audit PIK-1, PIK-14, PICKERS). New optional `recordLabel` names each row's checkbox and eye, the preview's Select checkbox and what previous and next say (it defaults to `recordTitle` when that returns a string); `recordCode` is the readable identifier over the preview's name, in place of the raw id; `previewColumn` names the column that names the record, which then leads the row and carries the eye. The eye comes from the table's own `preview`, so the naming column need not be a `c.id`. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Readable Names.
- **RecordBrowser says when there is nothing to link, and while its records load** (audit PIK-3, PICKERS). New optional `empty` (title, description, action, secondary, illustration) replaces the table when there are no eligible records, with the browser's `actions` as its step and the context kept above; the inverted "No matching records" copy is gone, and a search that matches nothing is the table's filtered state with Clear filters. New optional `state` (`loading`, `refreshing`, `error`) and `error` pass through to the DataTable. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Nothing To Link and Loading, failure and no match.
- **RecordBrowser holds the choice it is linking** (audit PIK-7, G4-16, PICKERS). While `onConfirm` runs the results are inert and `aria-busy`, and the preview's Select checkbox and Clear selection wait; Cancel is a subtle DialogClose under the Dialog's pending lock. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Pending confirmation and replacement sessions.
- **RecordBrowser's preview walks every page and says which record** (audit PIK-8, D6, PICKERS). The preview header uses PreviewNavigation without a full-record link: previous and next walk every filtered, sorted row and turn the table's page as they cross it, the status names the record with its position, the ends keep focus, and closing the preview returns focus to the eye of the record being read. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Preview across pages.
- **RecordBrowser's table fits its pane** (audit PIK-9, PICKERS). The DataTable is `responsive` and takes the height the dialog leaves it: the fields that do not fit beside an open preview move into More fields, and the rows scroll under the sticky header while the toolbar and the pagination stay in view. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Fits its pane.
- **RecordBrowser's way back leads on a phone** (audit PIK-19, PICKERS). Where the preview takes the results' place, Back to results is a leading chevron, away from the dialog's Close; beside the results it stays the X at the end. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Phone.
- **RecordBrowser keeps its exit animation** (audit PIK-20, PICKERS). The last session stays drawn through the dialog's exit and each opening starts afresh under its own key; a late completion from a closed session still closes and updates nothing. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Pending confirmation and replacement sessions.
- **RecordBrowser and PickerSheet keep focus clear of their footer in a short window** (audit G5-1, G5-6, PICKERS). Under 30rem tall a control that takes focus scrolls clear of the sticky footer, through DialogFooter's and SheetFooter's own scroll padding, and the RecordBrowser preview grows and scrolls with the dialog, so its name and its Select checkbox can each be seen. PickerSheet draws no description when it has no `subtitle`. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Short Window and Patterns/PickerSheet Short Window.
- **RecordBrowser's words are the locale's** (audit PIK-17, FDB-1, PICKERS). New optional `searchPlaceholder`; Search records, Back to results, Clear selection, the count, Cancel and the failure come from the LedgerProvider (`recordBrowser…`, `selectedCount`, `recordPositionNamed`), and a failed link is a danger Alert, not a bare paragraph. See Patterns/RecordBrowser Failed Link.
- **PickerSheet composes the kit's Toolbar** (audit TLB-10, PICKERS). The search and the filters are a Toolbar, so a narrow sheet folds the filters into More, which counts the table's filters that apply; the caller's `toolbar` row follows. See Patterns/PickerSheet Single choice.
