- **PickerSheet's count is a status, its Cancel is subtle, and one choice is named** (audit PIK-16, PIK-17, MOD-13, PIK-11, PICKERS). The footer's count is `role="status"`; Cancel is a subtle Button; a `table` that chooses one (`selectable: "single"`) reads the chosen row's `rowLabel` in place of "1 chosen". The stories' tables are `responsive` with a priority-0 statement, and PickerSheet.mdx says how large sets page. See Patterns/PickerSheet Picker sheet and Single choice.
- **RecordPicker returns focus, says what it finds and has its states** (audit PIK-6, PIK-13, G1-10, MNU-5, PICKERS). The field is no longer `autoFocus`, so focus goes back to the opener after Escape, Enter or a click. New optional `description` (a record-oriented sentence unsaid), `empty` (no records) apart from `emptyHint` (no match), `loading`, `error` and `onRetry` (a failed load with nothing listed shows no "0 matches" count); a record's optional `code` is shown in place of an `id` that is a database key, bounded so it truncates before the name. See Patterns/RecordPicker Record picker and States.

- **A textured Donut draws every slice** (audit CHO-1, CHARTB). The ring hands its patterns to the Plot's `textures`, so every `url()` fill finds its pattern and the four slices of a textured ring draw. See Patterns/Chart/Donut Donut Matrix, whose play resolves every fill.
- **A Donut has no dead tab stops, and its slices choose from the keyboard** (audit G3-9, CHO-2, D9, CHARTB). Neither Pie is a tab stop (`rootTabIndex={-1}`). A named ring that chooses nothing is `role="img"` with no tab stop; with `onSelect` or `details` each slice is a button named with its value and share ("Partial: 40, 11%"), `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded` when it opens a card, chosen with Enter or Space, its keyboard ring an arc in `color.border.focused` inside the slice. See Patterns/Chart/Donut Details.
- **Donut `max`, `value` and `tone`, and a gauge that is a meter** (audit CHO-8, CHO-9, CHARTB). `max` is the whole the slices are parts of, the rest the track, which neither hovers nor keys nor enters the legend or the table; `value` with `tone` is one value in place of `slices`. One value against `max` is `role="meter"` with `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax` and `aria-valuetext` ("72 of 100"). See Patterns/Chart/Donut Gauge.
- **A slice hidden from the legend keeps its place** (audit CHO-10, CHARTB). The whole stays every slice's sum, so the others keep their angles, the track shows through, and a share is always of the same whole. See Patterns/Chart/Donut Framed, whose play checks Satisfied's arc before and after.
- **A sliver still draws** (audit CHO-19, CHARTB). With more than one slice, a slice takes 3° at least, wider than its separators, so 1 of 372 can be hovered. See Patterns/Chart/Donut Small Share.
- **Donut `centerLabel`, Scatter `nameKey`** (audit CHO-12, CHARTB). The Donut's middle is `centerLabel`, and `label` for it is deprecated: in the next version `label` names the ring, as it names every other plot. Scatter's datum key is `nameKey`, and `name` for it is deprecated. See Patterns/Chart/Donut and Patterns/Chart/Scatter.
- **The Treemap's keyboard ring is drawn inside the tile** (audit CHO-3, G3-8, CHARTB). A 2px `color.border.focused` ring between two surface bands, the last thing the tile paints, so no later tile covers it, the svg's edge cannot cut it and a tile of the same hue does not hide it; `Highlight` in forced colours. A tile that opens a card says so (`aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`). See Patterns/Chart/Treemap Details, whose play checks the ring on the first and last tile.
- **The Treemap's tooltip names the tile** (audit CHO-11, CHARTB). Its name, the branch it sits in and its value, in place of "Value 3" beside a native title; the tooltip stands down while the keyboard is on a tile. See Patterns/Chart/Treemap Details.
- **A Treemap selection carries its path** (audit CHO-18, CHARTB). `TreemapSelection` adds `path` and `id`, and a node takes an optional `id`; a chosen tile is matched by `id`, else by path, so two components called Core in two systems stay apart. See Patterns/Chart/Treemap Same Names.
- **A Treemap or Scatter that chooses nothing is an image** (audit D9, CHARTB). Named and not choosing, its svg is `role="img"` with no tab stop; choosing, the plot is a group and its marks the tab stops. One that chooses with no name is named "Chart", with a warning in development. See Patterns/Chart/Treemap Treemap Matrix and Patterns/Chart/Scatter Scatter Matrix.
- **Scatter points choose from the keyboard** (audit CHO-2, D9, CHARTB). With `onSelect` or `details` each point is a button named with its group and every axis ("POA-102, Open: Days open 212 d, Exposure $420K"), chosen with Enter or Space, with a keyboard ring outside its surface ring; focus returns to it when its card closes. A plain point draws solid at 8px; a bubble at 70%. See Patterns/Chart/Scatter Details.
- **Scatter axes take a domain, ticks and a format each** (audit CHO-13, CHARTB). `xDomain` and `yDomain` (round ticks when both ends are pinned), `xTicks` and `yTicks`, and `formatY` and `formatZ` beside `formatX`, each used in the ticks, the tooltip, the card and the table; x no longer prints 12000 while y prints 12K, and the y axis is as wide as its longest label. See Patterns/Chart/Scatter Bubbles.
- **Points at the same place are listed together** (audit CHO-14, CHARTB). The tooltip and the card name every point at the place, and the card offers the others as buttons. The lead example plots two measures (days open by exposure) rather than two ordinal scales. See Patterns/Chart/Scatter Same Place.
- **A colour-scale Heatmap keeps its values in forced colours** (audit CHO-6, CHARTB). A cell (`data-slot="chart-heatmap-cell"`) and a Chart.Scale step (`chart-scale-step`) keep their fill, outlined in CanvasText, and an empty cell is a dashed GrayText box. See Patterns/Chart/Heatmap Heatmap Matrix in the forced-colours project.
- **Heatmap cells: no zero tab stops, a popup's state, the locale's words** (audit CHO-7, CHARTB). A cell of zero is not a button unless the new `selectable` says so; a cell that opens a card has `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded` in place of `aria-pressed`; "none" and "by" come from the new `chartNoValue`, `chartMarkIn` and `chartDimensions` messages. See Patterns/Chart/Heatmap Details.
- **The Heatmap takes the Frame's state and name** (audit CHO-15, CHARTB). Inside a Frame, `state="loading"` draws its skeleton cells, `aria-busy`, and `label` is the Frame's title when unsaid. See Patterns/Chart/Heatmap Loading.
- **Chart.Scale reads as the grid is binned** (audit CHO-16, CHARTB). Given the Heatmap's `domain`, a sequential key prints the value at each step's edge in `format`; `zeroStep`, on both, gives zero its own swatch in `color.chart.track`; `scale="status"` with `steps` keys a tone function's tones in words. `min` and `max` are optional. See Patterns/Chart/Heatmap Keys and Status.
- **A narrow Heatmap keeps its row names and equal columns** (audit CHO-17, CHARTB). The row headers stick at the start while the grid scrolls; the scroller is a named region and a tab stop only while it overflows; every value column takes the widest heading's width. See Patterns/Chart/Heatmap Narrow and Status.

- **Editable keeps a refused value** (audit EDT-2, G4-15's kit half, EDITING). A rejected `save` still rolls the committed value back and shows the reason, and now keeps what the reader wrote: the row offers Try again ("Try again to save Owner"), which sends it again, and Discard, which drops it, and reopening the field shows it. Both return focus to the row. See Patterns/Editable Failing.
- **Editable tells its host about drafts** (audit EDT-3, PA5-8's kit half, EDITING). `onEditingChange(editing)`, `onDraftChange(draft | null)` (the typed draft while it differs from the value, or a refused value; `null` once saved, put back, discarded or unmounted) and `onCancel()` on Editable.Text and Editable.Select, so a draft guard no longer reads the field's DOM events. All optional. See Patterns/Editable Failing.
- **Editable's Escape stops at the field** (audit EDT-6, EDITING). Escape in the field puts the old value back and no longer closes the Dialog, Sheet or Popover around it; the next Escape, from the row, does. See Patterns/Editable In Dialog.
- **Editable can be cancelled by touch, and a refused leave stays put** (audit EDT-7, EDITING). While editing, Cancel and Save show under the field as named xsmall icon buttons: always for `multiline`, wherever any pointer is coarse, and after the reader leaves a value `validate` refuses, which keeps the field open with its message. A press on either keeps focus in the field. See Patterns/Editable Validation and Multiline.
- **Switching windows keeps Editable's draft** (audit EDT-8, EDITING). A blur from leaving the window or the tab no longer commits: the field stays open with its draft and focus returns to it. See Patterns/Editable Validation.
- **A refused Editable row says why** (audit EDT-5, EDITING). After a failed save the Text row is `aria-invalid` and described by the reason, and the status says "Not saved: " and the reason (`editableNotSavedReason`). See Patterns/Editable Failing.
- **A multiline value is text with an Edit button** (audit EDT-9, EDITING). At rest a `multiline` Editable.Text is a paragraph the reader can select and copy, with a subtle xsmall Edit button ("Edit Statement") at its end, instead of one large button named by the whole text. See Patterns/Editable Multiline.
- **Editable.Select takes labelled options and an empty choice** (audit EDT-4, EDT-11, EDITING). `options` also takes `{ value, label }`: the list shows, searches and announces the label and commits the value, and `render(value, label)` receives both. `emptyLabel` adds a first choice that clears the value, and `placeholder` names an empty value at rest; unsaid, the muted dash, never a bare chevron. The `EditableOption` type is exported. See Patterns/Editable Options.
- **Editable's temporary lock** (audit EDT-10's kit half, EDT-17's kit half, EDITING). `lockedReason` keeps a row in place and focusable while another change saves: `aria-disabled`, described by the reason, and showing it when the reader tries to edit, so a host no longer disables a fieldset and drops focus. See Patterns/Editable Locked.
- **Editable is one height, exported by name and documented from its types** (audit EDT-18, A11-11's Editable half, EDITING). The resting row is `dimension.control.xsmall`, the field's 24px, so opening the field no longer moves the rail by 2px; its text aligns to the start in either direction. `EditableText` and `EditableSelect` are exported by name as well as through `Editable`, and Editable.mdx uses `<ArgTypes>` for both. The Roster play waits for the popup to take focus. See Patterns/Editable Rail.
- **Editable's words come from the LedgerProvider** (audit EDT-19's Editable half, EDITING). The multiline hint, Edit, Try again, Discard and the Cancel and Save names are messages (`editableMultilineHint`, `editableEdit`, `editableRetryNamed`, `editableDiscard`, `editableDiscardNamed`, `editableCancelNamed`, `editableSaveNamed`). See Patterns/Editable Multiline.
- **Composer returns focus after Send** (audit EDT-12, EDITING). Once a send lands and the draft clears, focus is back in the textarea instead of on the page, since Send is then unavailable. See Patterns/Composer Save Recovery.
- **Composer's suggestions are announced** (audit EDT-13, EDITING). The textarea has `aria-haspopup="listbox"`; a list that appears or changes length says its count and keys ("2 suggestions. Up and Down to choose, Enter or Tab to insert."), and each move names the option and its place ("Delivery, 2 of 2"), through `announce`. See Patterns/Composer Suggestions.
- **Composer's suggestion list floats in the kit's positioner** (audit EDT-14, EDITING). The list is portaled through Popover's positioner, anchored to the field with focus left in it: a clipping panel or dialog no longer cuts it, it opens above the field where the window ends below, and it is capped at the height the window leaves. Inside a Dialog it belongs to the dialog: choosing an option leaves the dialog open, and Escape closes the list first. See Patterns/Composer Suggestions At The Edge and Suggestions In Dialog.
- **Composer's active suggestion shows in forced colours** (audit EDT-15, EDITING). The options carry `data-slot="composer-option"` and the active one `data-highlighted`, which forced colours draw as Highlight with HighlightText. See Patterns/Composer Suggestions.
- **Composer's words and its failure** (audit EDT-19's Composer half, FDB-1's Composer half, EDT-16's kit half, EDITING). Send, Cancel, Suggestions, the failure and the keyboard hint default to LedgerProvider messages (`composerSend`, `composerSuggestions`, `composerSendFailed`, `composerSendHint`); the default hint is left out on a touch screen; `errorMessage` may be a function of the rejection, to show the server's reason. A rejected send's message is the textarea's description and is announced once, instead of a bare `role="alert"` paragraph. Composer.mdx no longer cites an application part. See Patterns/Composer Server Reason and Save Recovery.
- **Editable says a refusal once, and its lines under the value wrap** (audit EDT-5, EDT-10's kit half, EDITING review). The status says "Not saved: " and the reason the commit was refused for, and no longer changes to "Not saved" or another message as the reader types on. The message and the lock's reason wrap under a value that truncates (a KeyValue's, a table cell's) instead of being cut at its edge. Editable.Select shows an empty option of the caller's own as its `render` or the muted dash, and gives way to `emptyLabel` when both are set. See Patterns/Editable Validation, Locked, Failing and Options.
- **Composer's Shift+Tab leaves the field** (audit EDT-13, EDITING review). With the suggestions open, Tab and Enter insert the active one; Shift+Tab now moves back out of the field as it always does and closes the list, instead of inserting. The list keeps `overscroll-none`. See Patterns/Composer Suggestions.
- **An Editable in a KeyValue no longer reads as cut text** (audit EDT-1, EDT-6, EDITING review). The Editable keeps the reach of its tint, its field and its focus ring inside its own box at the end (`space.050`), so a truncating KeyValue value or cell no longer takes it for cut text: hovering or focusing the row no longer opens a tooltip repeating "Owner: Dana Whitfield", and Escape from the row reaches the Dialog around it instead of closing that tooltip. See Patterns/Editable Rail and In Dialog.
- **A saving paragraph's Edit button says it is unavailable** (audit EDT-10's kit half, EDITING review). While a `multiline` value saves, its Edit button keeps focus and is `aria-disabled` again; the lock's `aria-disabled` no longer overrides the Button's own. See Patterns/Editable Locked.

- **PreviewNavigation says which record arrived** (audit PRV-6, D6, PREVIEWKIT). `recordLabel` is said with the position ("Access review evidence, 2 of 6 records") when the preview opens and on each step; the status mounts empty and speaks once it has settled, so the first record is heard too. The controls are a group named "Record navigation" (`recordNavigation`). New messages: `recordPositionNamed`, `recordOutsideResultsNamed`, `recordNavigation`. See Patterns/PreviewNavigation Collection and In the panel.
- **PreviewNavigation's endpoints stay put** (audit PRV-7, PREVIEWKIT). A step that cannot run stays focused and says it is unavailable (`aria-disabled`) instead of turning disabled and sending focus to the other button: a repeated Enter at the last record stays there, Tab reaches both buttons, and Escape still reaches the panel or sheet. In forced colours the unavailable step is GrayText, as a native disabled button is. Assert `aria-disabled` rather than `toBeDisabled()`. See Patterns/PreviewNavigation Collection and Outside results.
- **PreviewNavigation shows its position and its reason** (audit PRV-5, PRV-12, PREVIEWKIT). `showPosition` draws "2 of 6" before the buttons and "Not in results" outside them (`recordPositionShort`, `recordNotInResults`). Outside the results both buttons say why in a tooltip that opens on hover, keyboard focus and a tap. Previous, Next and the full-record link show their names in tooltips; the link is a LinkIconButton. See Patterns/PreviewNavigation Visible position and Outside results.
- **PreviewNavigation's `openLink` is optional** (audit PIK-8's kit half, PREVIEWKIT). A preview inside a selection task leaves it out and gets previous, next and the announcement alone. See Patterns/PreviewNavigation In a selection task.
- **Preview chevrons and Back follow the reading direction** (audit PRV-14, PREVIEWKIT). PreviewNavigation's chevrons and PreviewSheet's Back turn in a right-to-left provider. See Patterns/PreviewNavigation Localized.
- **PreviewSheet: Close dismisses every frame, Back pops one and returns focus** (audit D6, PRV-2's kit half, PRV-11, PREVIEWKIT). The docs and the frame specimen show Close and Escape ending the whole preview from any frame. Back is an arrow, "Back to previous record" (`backToPreviousRecord`), at the start of the outer bar, apart from previous and next; the new `backFocus` names the control in the parent frame that opened the nested one, and focus goes there once the parent frame shows, else to the parent's title. See Patterns/PreviewSheet Preview sheet.
- **PreviewSheet's record title is PageHeader.Title** (audit PRV-9's PreviewSheet half, PREVIEWKIT). The inner title renders PageHeader.Title through the SheetTitle, the h2 that names the modal, instead of a copy of its classes. See Patterns/PreviewSheet Collection task.
- **PreviewSheet's `id` and `openTo` are optional where they mean nothing** (audit PRV-13, PREVIEWKIT). `id` may be left out; `openTo` may be left out when `navigation` is a PreviewNavigation carrying the full-record link, and is still required without navigation. The fallback name, "Open the full record", is the `openTheFullRecord` message. See Patterns/PreviewSheet Collection task and Preview sheet.
- **PreviewHeader is gone** (audit PRV-15, PREVIEWKIT). The unexported, unused `src/patterns/preview-header.tsx` is deleted; PreviewSheet's metadata block holds the compact identity row.

- **`grow="fill"` can shrink, and `shrink="none"` holds** (audit PRM-4, FOUNDATIONS). A Stack or Inline with `grow="fill"` drops its automatic minimum size on both axes (`flex-1 min-w-0 min-h-0`), so a one-line Text inside it cuts with an ellipsis instead of pushing the row past its frame; Box, Stack, Inline, Flex and Grid take `shrink="none"` for the fixed part beside it, an id or a count. See Primitives/Inline Grow Fill Truncates.
- **A wrapping meta line never starts or ends on its separator** (audit PRM-11, FOUNDATIONS). With `separator` and `shouldWrap`, each child and the separator after it wrap as one, and a separator that ends a line is hidden, measured again whenever the row or one of its children changes size, a child that loads its own name included; a `dl` joins `ul` and `ol` as a list that draws no separator, and the console says so once. See Primitives/Inline Wrapped Separator and List Semantics.
- **Text `numeric`, and tabular numerals in every table cell** (audit PRM-9's kit half, TOK-5's base half, FOUNDATIONS). `numeric` sets tabular numerals on a Text; `base.css` sets them on a table and its cells, so a type utility on the table no longer resets them. See Primitives/Text Numeric.
- **A long word breaks instead of widening the page** (audit G1-3, FOUNDATIONS). `base.css` sets `overflow-wrap: break-word` on the body, so an id, a hash or a URL longer than its line breaks where it would overflow a title, a description or a fact. See Primitives/Text Unbroken Word.
- **A one-line Text on a span cuts in a narrow frame, checked** (audit PRM-3, PRM-17, FOUNDATIONS). A play asserts the default span with `maxLines={1}` stays in its 200px frame with an ellipsis, alone and in a row, and `test/primitives.types.tsx` holds the primitives' promises at compile time: no link or button element, a required Heading size, token space and colour, no margin, no `style` where there is none. See Primitives/Text One Line On A Span.
- **A fieldset primitive stays in its frame** (audit PRM-6's kit half, FOUNDATIONS). `as="fieldset"` on Box, Stack, Inline, Flex or Grid drops the fieldset's content-wide minimum width; Box.mdx and Stack.mdx point a group of fields at FieldSet. See Primitives/Box.
- **Text entry at 16px on a coarse pointer** (audit TOK-8, INP-11, FOUNDATIONS). Wherever any pointer is coarse, a text-entry control's text is 1rem and never under 16px, so iOS Safari does not zoom the page into a focused field; the control keeps its token height. See Tokens/Typography Control Text On Touch in storybook-touch.
- **A chosen row keeps its words and its focus ring** (audit TOK-13, G3-7, FOUNDATIONS). `color.text.brand` is `color.blue.725` in light (4.95:1 on the selected fill, 4.70:1 hovered), `color.text.subtlest` and `color.icon.subtlest` take the new `color.darkNeutral.715` in dark (4.74:1 on the selected fill), and `color.border.focused` is `color.blue.600` in light, 3:1 on the neutral and selected fills a focused control sits inside. contrast.test declares these, the icons in a selected row and every chart series tone at 3:1 on its surfaces. See Tokens/Color Increased Contrast and Chart.
- **Layer tokens** (audit TOK-14, FOUNDATIONS). `layer.chrome` (30), `layer.blanket` (40), `layer.overlay` (50), `layer.toast` (1000) and `layer.tooltip` (1100), with the tooltip above the toast, as `--ds-layer-*`. See Tokens/Color Elevation.
- **Reduced motion stops turns and every-property transitions** (audit TOK-15, G8-1, G8-7, FOUNDATIONS). Under `prefers-reduced-motion`, `transition-transform` and `transition-all` land at once, wherever a part left them unguarded; Motion.mdx says what reduced motion does, a spinner stopping included. See Tokens/Motion Preference.
- **The token and primitive pages match the code** (audit PRM-16, PGL-18, DOC-10, FOUNDATIONS). Metrics and Grid no longer name the undefined `grid-cols-main-rail`; Space explains its names in 8px steps; Shape and the radius tokens name the parts each radius is on (`xlarge` has none); the Palette shows its eight ramps; Typography and the Heading page give each heading size the titles it really draws, and Stack and Inline say what `grow="fill"` and a default `alignBlock` do. See Tokens/Palette Ramps.

- **PageHeader is not a landmark** (audit PGL-2, PAGE). The root renders a `div` by default, so a header inside a PreviewSheet or a dialog is no longer a second banner and a page's top nav is its one banner; `render={<header />}` gives a page the element. `PageHeaderProps` is `useRender.ComponentProps<"header">`. A consumer that located the header by its tag finds it by `[data-slot="page-header"]`. See Layout/PageHeader Collection and Title levels.
- **PageHeader's stories and page follow the recipes** (audit PGL-13, PGL-14's docs half, PGL-18, PAGE). Collection is the register's name alone; Record is a trail Lead whose last level is the code, the name, and one Actions menu with a trailing chevron; Description appears only in With a description, for a page outside the register and record shapes. The page gains When to use, a table of which actions and sizes each header takes (a register none, a record one default-size primary or Actions menu, a preview one small primary and a subtle overflow), and an Accessibility section. See Layout/PageHeader.
- **A Section is a landmark only at the top of its outline** (audit PGL-4, VW1-21's Section half, PAGE). A titled Section whose title is an h2 is a region named by its title; under another titled Section, in a titled panel, in a dialog or under a preview's record title it is a titled block in the heading outline only, so a record's landmark list names its parts and a preview adds none. `landmark` decides it outright; an untitled Section is never one. Nested Sections that were regions are not any more. See Layout/Section Landmarks and Nested.
- **Section's header reads as a header** (audit PGL-5, PAGE). Section.Title is semibold at body size, a one-line title sits centred on its action's row, and `divided` draws its `color.border` rule inside the header's end, `space.100` above the content, so the content never touches it and keeps its own flow (inline content stays one line); a collapsible Section ends at its rule while closed. See Layout/Section Presentation and Collapsible.
- **PageSkeleton takes the page's shape and says so once** (audit PGL-7, PGL-6's kit half, FDB-13's PageSkeleton half, PAGE). `variant` is `register` (the default: the title with nothing under it, the toolbar's row, the table's header and rows, as DataTable's loading rows) or `record` (the trail, the title with its action, a tab strip and two sections). `label` ("Loading page", the `loadingPage` message) is sent once through the kit's persistent polite live region and kept as visually hidden text in the `status` root, which no longer carries `aria-busy`; in forced colours every shape is `GrayText`. See Layout/PageSkeleton Register, Record and Page Skeleton Matrix.
- **fill-window counts the toolbar, and the layout utilities use the dynamic viewport** (audit PGL-9, PAGE). A filling register is never shorter than its toolbar's row, a header, six rows and the pagination, so the smallest register shows six rows; `sticky-rail` and `min-h-work` measure `100dvh`, as `fill-window` does. See Layout/Pages Register.
- **Focus scroll padding belongs to the page** (audit G5-2, PAGE). The global `scroll-margin-block` on every focused control is gone; the document's own scroll padding keeps what focus scrolls to clear of the sticky banner and top nav (their height and `space.200`, `space.1000` at the bottom, `space.200` under 30rem of height), so a dialog, a sheet, a panel or a one-row list brings a focused row wholly into its own view. See Layout/Pages Focus stays in view.
- **Layout/Pages renders the recipes** (audit PGL-13, PRV-8's story half, PAGE). Record: a trail Lead, the name, an Actions menu whose destination is a link item, Sections with Prose, the Details rail as an Inspector group of Editables and KeyValues, and an Evidence tab that is a register. Register: the name alone, a filling `responsive` DataTable under a Toolbar with saved views, a filter, Columns, Settings, the collection's overflow and one small primary, TextLink names, and a failed refresh as one Alert with Retry above the rows. Queue with panel: the eye opens a panel named by its `label`, whose outer header is PreviewNavigation and Close only and whose body starts with the record's PageHeader h2 beside a small primary and an overflow; previous and next walk `displayedRows` with `showRow`. The page documents filling the window (`fill-window`, `useFillWindow`) and the focus scroll padding. See Layout/Pages.
- **Breadcrumb's name, direction and focus** (audit NAV-11, NAV-12, NAV-8, NAV-2's kit warning, NAV-15's Breadcrumb half, PAGE). The landmark is named "Breadcrumb" by default (the `breadcrumb` message); BreadcrumbEllipsis loses its unreachable hidden "More". The default chevrons turn in a right-to-left trail. A level that folds while it holds focus hands focus to the ellipsis without scrolling the page. A trail that marks more than one level, or a level other than its last, as the current page says so once per trail in the console ("Ledger: a breadcrumb trail marks …"). The page drops its migration table and its duplicate PageHeader link. See Components/Breadcrumb Right to left, Collapses To Fit and Dont.
- **Resizable from the package root, without dragging and right to left** (audit NAV-14, NAV-6, NAV-7's docs half, PAGE). `useResizableLayout` (the upstream `useDefaultLayout`) and the `ResizablePanelGroupHandle`, `ResizablePanelHandle`, `ResizableLayout` and `ResizableLayoutStorage` types are exported, so persisting or driving a split imports nothing from react-resizable-panels. The handle takes a 24px hit area on a touch screen. The page requires a way to change a split without dragging (WCAG 2.5.7) and shows a fold button on `collapse()` and `expand()`, says how a split behaves in a narrow container, and that a group set right to left before it mounts moves its handle the way the arrow points, which the library already does. See Components/Resizable Without dragging, Right to left and Persisted.

- **A Stepper's current step stays a button** (audit STR-2, STRUCTURE). While any step takes `onSelect`, the current step is a button too, carrying `aria-current="step"` itself, so the step the reader activates keeps focus as it becomes current; activating it runs its own `onSelect` or nothing. Each step's name is its state, then its label and meta ("Current: Implement"); the drawn number is not read. Stepper.mdx gains the wizard's focus rule: a step change from outside the rail moves focus to the new step's heading. See Components/Stepper Wizard and Paths.
- **A Stepper's labels reveal when cut, and the path ahead reads** (audit STR-20, STR-19, STRUCTURE). A label is cut to one line and shows whole on hover and while its step has keyboard focus. An upcoming ring is `color.border.bold` and the rail behind a done step `color.border.success`; contrast.test holds that rail at 3:1 on the page and raised surfaces. Stepper.mdx is rewritten in the present tense to match. See Components/Stepper Dont and Stepper Matrix.
- **Stepper and Timeline keep the list role** (audit STR-17, STRUCTURE). Their `ol` elements, Timeline.Group's list included, say `role="list"`, which WebKit drops from a list without markers, so VoiceOver keeps "3 of 6". See Components/Stepper Stepper Matrix and Components/Timeline Timeline Matrix.
- **A horizontal Stepper or Timeline is a tab stop only while it scrolls** (audit STR-6, NAV-5, G3-1, STRUCTURE). The hard-coded `tabIndex={0}` is gone: the strip is a stop only while it overflows and holds nothing the keyboard reaches, then a group named after the list ("Releases, scrolls", the locale's `namedContentScrolls`), and a focused step's or event's ring is drawn inside the strip. See Components/Stepper Narrow and Components/Timeline Narrow and Stages.
- **A Tree row is named by its label and keeps to its line** (audit STR-3, STR-4, STRUCTURE). The label is cut to one line and shows whole on hover and while its row has keyboard focus; the row is `aria-labelledby` its label, so an arrow-key move reads "Finance", with the hint and the text or Count in `trailing` as its description and the row's controls in neither. Text in `trailing` is its own tooltip on hover, so the row's focus reveals only the name. See Components/Tree Long names and Families.
- **Tree.Item `hint` and `actions`** (audit STR-5, PA1-2's kit half, STRUCTURE). `hint` is muted text after the label (a code, a kind, a profile) that takes only the room the label leaves and is cut first, and its words, a composed hint's included, are its own tooltip, so the row's focus reveals only the name; `actions` holds the row's controls, each its own tab stop; text in `trailing` gives way beside the label instead of taking the name's room. See Components/Tree Long names.
- **A click on a Tree row's Count or Badge selects the row** (audit STR-15, STRUCTURE). The trailing slot no longer stops the click; a control or focusable element inside the row keeps its own. See Components/Tree Families.
- **Read-only Tree rows carry no selection state, and the arrows follow the reading direction** (audit STR-16, STRUCTURE). A row takes `aria-selected` only with `onSelect` or `isSelected`, and the hover tint and pointer cursor only with `onSelect`; in a right-to-left tree Left opens and Right closes. The chevron rests under reduced motion (audit TOK-15, the Tree part). See Components/Tree Read Only and Selected and right to left.
- **A Timeline says which event is open and which are unread** (audit STR-8, STRUCTURE). `isActive` puts `aria-current="true"` on the row's link or button, or on the row when it does not open; the new `isUnread` sets the title in weight 500 and reads "Unread" before it (the locale's `timelineUnread`), where `emphasis` stays a visual weight for the current event. See Components/Timeline Activity and Timeline Matrix.
- **A Timeline's full stamp is read, and relative time is built in** (audit STR-10, STRUCTURE). `timeTitle` is visually hidden text after the time as well as its tooltip. With an ISO `dateTime` and no `time`, the time is the relative words in the reader's locale, kept current, and with no `timeTitle` the full stamp is that moment in the reader's locale and zone. See Components/Timeline Relative times.
- **A Timeline stamp never crowds out the title** (audit G1-6, STRUCTURE). Beside the title a stamp takes at most half the line, in the column before the rail at most 160px, and is cut first with the full stamp as its reveal; a cut title shows whole on hover and while its row has keyboard focus. Timeline.mdx points long stamps at `timePosition="below"`. See Components/Timeline Long content.
- **Timeline.Group's heading keeps the outline, and its label rides over the markers** (audit STR-13, STR-14, STRUCTURE). The group label is a heading at the contextual level, an h3 outside every HeadingLevelProvider, and its sticky header sits above the marker column, so markers and the rail pass under it. See Components/Timeline Group headings and Timeline Matrix.
- **Timeline `itemWidth`, and `wrap` across** (audit STR-7's kit half, STRUCTURE). `itemWidth="rail"` gives each event across at least `dimension.layout.rail`, so stage titles and dates stay readable and the strip scrolls past that; `wrap` lets titles across wrap; a caller's `style` still overrides the minimum. See Components/Timeline Stages.
- **A Timeline's open event survives forced colours** (audit STR-9, STRUCTURE). forced-colors.css draws the event with `isActive` in Highlight with HighlightText inside, as the selected Tree row; the marker slot carries `data-slot="timeline-marker"`. See Components/Timeline Activity in storybook-forced-colors.
- **Step and event rings clear what they surround** (audit G3-1, STRUCTURE). Across, a start-aligned event keeps its marker and text `space.050` in from the column's start, the rail running on to the marker, so the ring drawn inside the strip passes beside them instead of over the first letter; down the page, a step's ring stops above the gap under it and no longer crosses the next step's marker. An icon marker keeps its disc as an outline in forced colours. See Components/Timeline Stages and Runs, and Components/Stepper Stepper Matrix.

- **An open panel keeps the register in view from 1024px** (audit SHB-6, D12, SHELLNAV). The panel is a column beside Main from the large breakpoint (`dimension.breakpoint.lg`), where it replaced Main below the panel breakpoint. Between the two an open panel shows the side nav collapsed, as its icon rail or hidden, and closing it gives the side nav back as the reader left it; the preference `persist` stores never changes, and a reader who expands the side nav meanwhile keeps it expanded until the panel closes. Below `lg` the panel replaces Main as before. `panelCompactQuery` follows `lg`; the shell root carries `data-sidenav` (`expanded`, `collapsed`, `overlay`) and `data-sidenav-yielded`. See Layout/Shell Panel beside Main at 1200px.
- **The phone side nav is modal** (audit SHA-1, G2-1, SHELLNAV). Below the large breakpoint, opening the overlay moves focus to the current page's item, else the first link; Tab and Shift+Tab go round inside it; the Banner, the top nav, Main, Aside, Panel and the skip links are `inert` until it closes. Its own Close sits where the toggle is, since the overlay covers the toggle. The blanket is `aria-hidden` and out of the tab order. Close, Escape and the blanket return focus to the toggle once the shell takes input again; a menu or a dialog opened from inside the overlay (an account menu's Settings) keeps its own Escape and Tab, and closing it leaves the overlay open. See Layout/Shell Overlay focus at 390px.
- **onCollapse and onExpand say which side nav changed** (audit SHA-7, SHA-3's mode half, SHELLNAV). They receive `SideNavChange`, `{ trigger, isOverlay }`, fire only when the state changes (a panel that shows the side nav collapsed calls neither; a collapse meanwhile, which changes the remembered preference, calls `onCollapse`), report the real trigger (the shortcut and the hook as well as the toggle) and emit `"viewport"` when the window grows past `lg` with the overlay open. `useSideNav().isOverlay` says whether the side nav is the phone overlay, so a product never repeats the breakpoint. A callback typed `({ trigger })` still fits. See Layout/Shell Overlay focus at 390px.
- **The skip link to the side nav is there only while it shows** (audit SHA-4, SHELLNAV). "Skip to Side navigation" is left out on a phone and while the side nav is collapsed to hidden, where it moved focus nowhere; in the icon rail it stays. See Layout/Shell Skip links.
- **The current page stands apart from hover, in the rail too** (audit SHA-5, SHA-6, G3-7, SHELLNAV). SideNav.Item's current page takes the selected fill, text and icon instead of hover's neutral tint. SideNav.Expandable takes `isActive`: it opens by default when it holds the current page, and its row takes the current colour while the group is closed and in the icon rail. In the rail a `badge` stays in the item's name and a brand dot marks the icon. In forced colours the current group row takes Highlight, the dot a system colour and the default Mark an edge. A side nav row's focus ring sits flush on its edge, on the side nav's surface; contrast.test.mjs pairs the selected fill, the ring and the dot with the sunken surface. See Layout/Shell Current page.
- **A cut side nav label shows its whole name** (audit SHA-12, G1-10's side-nav half, SHELLNAV). SideNav.Item, SideNav.Expandable and Profile show their name in a tooltip wherever the side nav's width cuts it, as they do in the icon rail. See Layout/Shell Current page.
- **Profile is named by the person** (audit SHA-14, SHB-13, SHB-14, STS-9, VW3-20, SHELLNAV). Profile hides its avatar from assistive technology, so the button reads "Sarah Chen Compliance lead", not the initials as well; `name` is declared on ProfileProps and shows in the props table. The chevron shows only while the button opens a menu (`aria-haspopup="menu"`, which a DropdownMenuTrigger sets), and `indicator` decides otherwise; a Profile that opens a dialog sets `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and has none. See Layout/Shell Frame and Forwarding.
- **SideNav's `defaultWidth` is never remembered** (audit SHA-13, SHELLNAV). It sets `--shell-sidenav-default` while the side nav is mounted, instead of writing itself as the reader's width, so `persist` stores only a width the reader dragged or keyed and a changed default reaches every reader who has not resized. See Layout/Shell Default widths.
- **The top nav's start slot moves with the side nav** (audit SHA-9, SHELLNAV). Collapsing or expanding the icon rail eases the start slot between its own width and the side nav's on the same curve, where it jumped to its end width on the first frame; reduced motion switches at once. See Layout/Shell Start slot motion.
- **A banner that wraps pushes the shell down by its height** (audit SHB-7's shell half, SHELLNAV). The banner area has the banner token as its minimum height, and the shell measures it and publishes its real height as `--shell-banner`, so the top nav, the side nav and the panel sit under a banner that wraps on a phone. See Layout/Shell Banner at 390px.
- **In a short window the banner and the top nav scroll with the page** (audit G5-8's shell half, SHELLNAV). Under 30rem of height they are no longer sticky and `--shell-top` is 0, so a phone on its side or a page zoomed to 400% keeps its height for the page. See Layout/Shell Short window.
- **The Details rail sits beside Main while the side nav is collapsed** (audit VW2-14, VW3-17, SHELLNAV). From the large breakpoint, a collapsed side nav (the icon rail or hidden) leaves the room Aside needs, so Aside sits beside Main below the aside breakpoint too instead of after a long page; beside Main its divider runs the page's height. See Layout/Shell Record rail at 1100px.
- **Shell uses the kit's tooltip timing** (audit FLT-10's Shell half, SHELLNAV). Shell's TooltipProvider takes the kit's defaults, 300ms to the first tooltip and 300ms to the next, instead of setting its own. See Layout/Shell Frame.
- **Shell.mdx documents accessibility and persistence** (audit SHA-15, SHA-10's docs half, SHELLNAV). The page says the root's children are its areas and anything else portals. An Accessibility section covers the landmarks, the skip links, the phone overlay's focus, Profile's name and the banner; a Persistence section says what `persist` stores, that `defaultWidth` and a yielded side nav are never stored, and that `persist={true}` shares one key, `ledger.shell`, across every Shell on the origin. See Layout/Shell docs.
- **Alert's role follows its tone** (audit FDB-5, API-6, FEEDBACK). A danger Alert is `role="alert"`; every other tone is `role="status"`, where every Alert was `role="alert"`. `role` chooses another (`note` for standing guidance) and `role={undefined}` none; every kit and app Alert already passes its role. `variant="danger"` is accepted as the Ledger spelling of `destructive`. Alert.mdx's Accessibility section is a decision table: failure after an act, update, standing note, said by the page. See Components/Alert Variants.
- **AlertIcon** (audit FDB-6, FEEDBACK). New part: the tone's icon first in the Alert (Info, CircleCheck, TriangleAlert, CircleAlert, as Banner's and the toast list's), so severity is a shape as well as a colour; `icon` replaces it. It is the tone's icon colour, and `color.icon.subtle` beside a neutral Alert's subtle text, so it holds 3:1 on every tone's fill in every mode, which contrast.test checks with the ring and a link on the same fills. In forced colours an Alert keeps a CanvasText edge inside its box. See Components/Alert Tones and Playground.
- **A link in an Alert reads as a link, and a long value wraps** (audit FDB-2, G1-4, FEEDBACK). AlertAction underlines its links as the title and the description do, and the anatomy shows a TextLink to navigate and a small Button to act; AlertTitle and AlertDescription break an unbroken code or hash (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) instead of widening the page. See Components/Alert Variants and Long content.
- **The feedback parts keep their identity** (audit API-9, FEEDBACK). Alert and its parts, the Empty parts, Skeleton, Spinner, the Progress parts and the toast parts set `data-slot` after the caller's props, so a caller can no longer replace it; EmptyIllustration is always hidden from assistive technology. ProgressStacked takes a span's native props and ref. See Components/Alert With Action and Components/Skeleton Native Attributes.
- **Banner wraps on a phone** (audit FDB-12, SHB-7's Banner half, G1-10, FEEDBACK). The bar is its own container: from 42rem wide it is one 48px line, and a message it cuts has its whole text as its title; narrower, the message wraps under the icon with the action after its last word and the bar grows with it, so a phone reads the whole message. In forced colours the bar's edge is drawn inside it and keeps its height. See Components/Banner Long message and Banner Matrix.
- **A toast with an action stays; the Toaster's default is five seconds** (audit FDB-10, FDB-11, FEEDBACK). The kit's `toast`, `createToastManager` and `useToastManager` give a toast with `actionProps` and no `timeout` a timeout of 0, so its action stays reachable through F6, and an error eight seconds; an explicit `timeout` wins. Toaster's default `timeout` is 5000, not 4000. Toaster.mdx says every toast action also exists on the page, that errors belong inline, and why the kit does not set Base UI's `priority: "high"`. See Components/Toaster With Action and Defaults.
- **Toast types in the kit's words** (audit FDB-15, FEEDBACK). `ToastType` is the union of Base UI's types and the kit's `information` and `danger`, which the manager stores as `info` and `error` so they draw their icons; `ToastOptions`, `ToastUpdateOptions`, `ToastPromiseOptions` and `ToastManager` type it, and `type: "danger"` no longer renders without an icon. `useToastManager`'s `add`, `update` and `promise` keep their identity while toasts come and go, as Base UI's do, so an effect can depend on them. See Components/Toaster Defaults.
- **Toast text can be copied** (audit FDB-16, FEEDBACK). ToastTitle and ToastDescription select as text and carry Base UI's swipe-ignore, so an error message or a record's code can be copied and a swipe that starts on them does not dismiss. See Components/Toaster Defaults.
- **The toast stack fits a short window** (audit G5-7, FEEDBACK). Under 30rem of height the Toaster shows two toasts, so the open stack stays inside the window, and a toast past the limit leaves the page once it has faded. See Components/Toaster Stack in storybook-short.
- **An unknown wait is a hatched bar that stands still under reduced motion** (audit FDB-7, FDB-8, FEEDBACK). `value={null}` draws the track's width in moving stripes of the tone's icon colour (one tile every `motion.duration.slower`) instead of a pulsing full bar, and the stripes stop under reduced motion; its `aria-valuetext` is the locale's new `progressIndeterminate` ("In progress"). See Components/Progress Values and ranges.
- **A warning bar holds 3:1** (audit G6-6, FEEDBACK). Progress and ProgressStacked draw a warning fill in `color.chart.warning` instead of `color.background.warning.bold`; contrast.test holds each tone's fill and each stripe colour to 3:1 on the track over the page, raised and overlay surfaces in both modes (the light warning orange on the sunken surface is 2.97:1 and stays open). See Components/Progress Tones.
- **A clickable ProgressStacked segment shows its whole ring and a touch target** (audit FDB-9, FEEDBACK). The bar no longer clips its segments (the end segments carry its rounding), a focused segment draws the kit's ring, in Highlight under forced colours, a segment's hit area is 24px tall on a coarse pointer, and in a group of buttons a segment that does not click is read as its title. See Components/Progress Stacked coverage.
- **Progress, Spinner and Toaster are on the template** (audit FDB-17, FDB-14's docs half, FEEDBACK). Each page says when to use the part and when another, its content rules and its Don'ts; Progress separates a task's progressbar from a share of a whole, Spinner recommends a 400ms delay for a wait that is often shorter and sends a long background task to the Button's `isLoading` and Attachment. See Components/Progress Dont, Components/Spinner Dont and Components/Toaster Dont.
- **A Spinner says its name when it appears** (audit FDB-13, FEEDBACK). A status inserted with its name set is announced by almost no screen reader, so a Spinner sends its name once, politely, through the kit's live regions as it appears, after its delay; spinners appearing together under one name are said once, and a decorative one says nothing. See Components/Spinner Announced.
- **The Spinner page renders without a React error** (FEEDBACK). The Playground's ref is set inside a story component rather than passed through the story's args, so the docs source no longer reads a ref from an element, which React 19 reports; the Don't's Skeleton example names its wait. See Components/Spinner Playground.
- **A skeleton's wait is said in words** (audit FDB-13's Skeleton half, FEEDBACK). Skeleton.mdx and its examples put a line that names the wait in the busy region, since `aria-busy` alone says nothing; in forced colours the shapes are GrayText. See Components/Skeleton Skeleton Matrix.
- **Empty keeps long values inside its frame** (audit G1-2, FEEDBACK). EmptyTitle and EmptyDescription break an unbroken token where they must and never exceed the message's measure, so a hash no longer widens the centred block past both edges. See Components/Empty Long content.
- **Every Empty picture paints its front surface on top** (audit EMP-4, EMP-16, FEEDBACK). The `document` scene's front page sits over the page behind it, each scene marks its front surface (`data-part="front"`), and the Illustrations play checks every kind's size, that it is hidden, and what is topmost at its front surface's centre; the Compact play checks the columns. See Components/Empty Illustrations and Compact.
- **Empty pictures keep their drawing in forced colours** (audit EMP-14, FEEDBACK). A scene's surfaces are Canvas with a CanvasText edge, its ghost lines and bars GrayText and a badge a CanvasText disc with a Canvas glyph; an icon EmptyMedia keeps its circle's edge. See Components/Empty Illustrations in storybook-forced-colors.
- **Empty says when a part is misplaced** (audit EMP-11, EMP-8, FEEDBACK). An EmptyMedia inside EmptyHeader, and an EmptyIllustration in a compact Empty, each say so once in the browser console, a warning that never stops the Empty rendering; Empty.mdx states the part order for both sizes. See Components/Empty Compact.
- **Empty's not-found and failed-to-load shapes** (audit EMP-9, EMP-15, EMP-10, EMP-8's docs half, FEEDBACK). New stories for a missing record (the search picture, the title as the page's h1, a link back) and a region that failed with nothing else to show (an icon, what failed, Try again); Empty.mdx adds both to its table, gives "waiting on someone else" an icon instead of `done`, and says a Section header's create action is not repeated in the Empty under it. See Components/Empty Not found, Failed to load and Dont.

- **A panel's `defaultWidth` is its own and is never remembered** (audit SHB-3, SHELLPANEL). Shell.Panel sets its `defaultWidth` as the shell's `--shell-panel-default` while it is open and clears it on close, instead of writing it as the reader's width, so each register's preview opens at its own default and nothing is stored until the reader drags or keys a width, which then outranks every default. A default under 240px opens at 240px. See Layout/Shell Panel widths.
- **A splitter step or drag lands at once** (audit SHB-5, PRF-8, SHELLPANEL). A key step holds the shell's column transition for the frames that draw it (`data-shell-resizing`), and a drag follows the pointer on the root's own style, so the panel is at the pointer's width as it moves and `aria-valuenow` says the new width as the key is pressed; the columns still ease as the panel opens and closes. See Layout/Shell Panel widths.
- **The splitters follow the window-splitter pattern** (audit SHB-18, A11-17's splitter half, SHELLPANEL). Shift with an arrow, and Page Up and Page Down, step 64px; Enter collapses the side nav, as a double-click does; each separator names the area it resizes with `aria-controls`; `onResizeStart` and `onResizeEnd` report only a key that changed the width, never End at the maximum, while the key still keeps the width it lands on. See Layout/Shell Panel widths and Frame.
- **The splitter shows in forced colours** (audit SHB-8, SHELLPANEL). The handle's band keeps a system colour, Highlight while it is hovered, focused or dragged, where forced colours dropped its brand fill and left no outline. See Layout/Shell Panel widths in the forced-colours project.
- **Escape in the panel leaves popups, composition and search fields alone** (audit SHB-10, SHELLPANEL). The panel closes on Escape only from inside its own element: not from a menu, list or dialog it opened (portalled out of it), not while an input method is composing, and not from a text or search field, a textarea, a select or an editable region; a tooltip open in the panel takes the first Escape. See Layout/Shell Panel focus, which now opens a menu in the panel, and Table in a panel, where Escape clears a DataTable's toolbar search and the panel stays.
- **A banner spans the panel's column, and the panel's header sits level with the top nav** (audit SHB-15, SHELLPANEL). The banner row covers the whole width and the panel beside the page starts under it, so the panel's header and the top nav share one row and their rules meet with or without a banner. See Layout/Shell With banner and panel.
- **The panel's scroll padding is its header's height** (audit SHB-16, SHELLPANEL). Panel.Header publishes its measured height as `--shell-panel-header`, and the panel keeps that and `space.200` clear under it, so a control that takes focus never stops under a header whose title took its own row. See Layout/Shell Panel header · 240px.
- **Close and the splitter are named after the panel** (audit SHB-17, SHELLPANEL). With a `label`, Panel.Close defaults to "Close {label}" and Panel.Splitter to "Resize {label}" (the locale's `closePanelNamed` and `resizePanelNamed`); without one they stay "Close details" and "Resize details", and their `label` props still override. See Layout/Shell Panel record header · 240px and Panel widths.
- **In a short window the panel's header scrolls with its content** (audit G5-8's panel half, SHELLPANEL). Under 30rem of height the header is no longer sticky and the scroll padding drops to `space.200`, so a phone on its side or a page zoomed to 400% gives the content the height. Beside the page, once the banner has scrolled away, the panel holds the window's whole height, as the side nav does, instead of leaving the banner's height empty above it. See Layout/Shell Panel in a short window and Panel in a short wide window.
- **Shell.mdx says what the panel does** (audit SHB-19, G2-4, SHELLPANEL). The page documents Escape's exceptions, the panel as the page's region where it replaces Main, `defaultWidth` as a fallback, the header's scroll padding and short-window rule, the splitter's keys, `aria-controls` and resize events, and names `LedgerProvider` for the default names. Panel.Title now registers through the shared `useRegisterTitle`.

- **A linked Stat.Tile** (audit STA-1, CNT-6, CARDS). `link` on Stat.Tile takes a router Link or `<a href>` and fills the tile with it, so the whole tile is one target named by its label, number and note, the tile stays Stat.Grid's child, it hovers and presses on `elevation.surface.hovered` and `.pressed`, and its focus ring is drawn inside the tile, where the frame cannot clip it. Stat.mdx no longer says to wrap a tile in a link. See Components/Stat Linked.
- **Stat loads and says so** (audit STA-4, CARDS). `isLoading` on Stat and Stat.Tile puts a heading-sized Skeleton in the number's line, which keeps its height so nothing moves when the number arrives, marks the part `aria-busy` and reads the locale's "Loading" after the label. Stat.mdx documents the unavailable state as an Absent with its reason and a note. See Components/Stat Loading and unavailable.
- **Stat writes numbers in the reader's locale** (audit STA-10, CARDS). A number `value` is formatted with the LedgerProvider's `formatNumber` (1,234 in en-US, 1.234 in de-DE); a string shows as given. See Components/Stat Numbers in the reader's locale.
- **Stat.Tile takes a trend beside its number** (audit STA-16, CARDS). `trend` holds a Chart.Sparkline `space.150` from the value, so the value stays the number, formats in the locale and a zero still reads muted. Stat.mdx's "Not built" paragraph loses its stale references (STA-15). See Components/Stat With a trend.
- **Stat.Grid keeps its hairlines in forced colours** (audit STA-17, CARDS). The gutter paint moves from an inline style to stat.css, so a caller's `style` still replaces it; in forced colours the grid paints the gaps in CanvasText and each tile sits on Canvas. Stat, Stat.Tile and Stat.Grid name themselves with `data-slot` (`stat`, `stat-tile`, `stat-grid`), and `StatTile` and `StatGrid` are exported by name. The Frames story's bare row uses a container-sized Grid instead of a viewport breakpoint (STA-11). See Components/Stat Stat matrix and Frames.
- **A linked Card** (audit CNT-6, CARDS). `link` on CardTitle makes the title's text a link stretched over the card: a click anywhere on it opens the link, the card hovers and presses on `elevation.surface.raised.hovered` and `.pressed`, and the ring is drawn inside the card's edge; a card that holds linked cards does not take their hover. A linked card holds no other control. See Components/Card Linked.
- **CardTitle takes `render`** (audit CNT-17, CARDS). `render={<h3 />}` makes the title the heading, as Section.Title's `render` does; the div default is unchanged. Every Card part now names itself with `data-slot` after the prop spread (CNT-16). See Components/Card Linked.
- **Item says which row is open, and what tells rows apart** (audit CNT-3, REC-11, CARDS). `isActive` puts `aria-current="true"` on the title's link or button, and the id, the meta and the description become that control's accessible description, so WorkPane.Row and the tailoring pickers inherit both. In forced colours, where the selected fill is removed, the open row is outlined in Highlight inside its edge. See Components/Item Open row and description.
- **Item reveals cut names, and its flush ring stays inside the card** (audit CNT-10, CNT-9, G1-10, CARDS). A cut title, id or meta shows in full in a tooltip (Truncate), a cut title also on keyboard focus of its row; `maxTitleLines` gives a title two or three lines; Item.Group's heading wraps. A flush row's focus ring is drawn inside its edge, so Card's clipping no longer leaves only its top and bottom lines. See Components/Item Long names.
- **Item.Group and Related see through fragments** (audit CNT-11, CARDS). A list whose children are empty arrays, `false` or empty fragments shows its `empty` instead of an empty list. See Components/Item Empty from several sources and Patterns/Related Nothing linked.
- **Related.Card's actions stay while their menu is open** (audit REC-9, TOO-8, CARDS). The actions key off Base UI's `data-popup-open` (the Radix `data-state` is kept), show wherever there is no hover or any pointer is coarse, and hovering one card no longer reveals every card's actions. See Patterns/Related Actions while a menu is open.
- **Related's words and names** (audit REC-20, G1-10, API-8, CARDS). The default empty title is the locale's new `relatedEmpty` message; the heading wraps; a card's cut name and meta reveal in full; `RelatedCard`, `RelatedCardProps`, `RelatedEmpty` and `RelatedLayout` are exported by name. See Patterns/Related Nothing linked and Long names.
- **CardHeader keeps the title's measure beside a wide action** (audit G1-5, CARDS). The header measures its title and its action and, as Section.Header does, keeps the title's readable measure (12rem, or the title's own width when shorter) beside the action; when it cannot, the action takes its own row after the description, at the end, so a title no longer collapses to a letter a line beside three buttons. A resized card follows. See Components/Card Beside a wide action.
- **An Item's meta keeps its words** (docs/next.md "A row's meta in a narrow list", CARDS). Beside a meta the title gives way first: a short meta ("CAT I") stays whole, and a long one is cut, with its reveal, only past half the line. See Components/Item Long names.
- **The contrast gate reads the linked tile and card fills** (audit STA-1, CNT-6, CARDS). `contrast.test.mjs` pairs the label, the note, a toned or zero count and the focus ring with `elevation.surface.hovered` and `.pressed`, and the card's text and ring with `elevation.surface.raised.hovered` and `.pressed`, in all four modes. See Components/Stat Linked and Components/Card Linked.
- **Card's sizes are `medium` and `small`** (audit CNT-16, CARDS). Card's `size` takes the words the kit's controls use, `medium` by default and `small`, and reports them in `data-size`; `default` and `sm` are its deprecated spellings for one version and render the same. `CardSize` is exported. See Components/Card Sizes.
- **Item's titles start at the start edge** (audit A11-11's Item half, CARDS). A title that is a button or a disclosure aligns `text-start` instead of `text-left`, so in a right-to-left document it sits at the right, as a link's does. See Components/Item Right to left.

- **TextLink opens a new tab and says so** (audit GAP-13, CNT-7). `newTab` sets `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"` (a `target` or `rel` of the caller's wins), puts an external-link icon after the last word, joined to it so a wrapping link never leaves it alone, and ends the link's name with "(opens in a new tab)", the locale's new `opensInNewTab`. It works the same through `render` on a router link. TextLink.mdx says when a new tab is justified, and that in a row field a `newTab` link sits inside the Truncate so its icon is cut with the words. See Components/TextLink New Tab.
- **A link in small subtle text is covered** (audit CNT-1, G2-2). TextLink's In Prose story adds a link in `font.body.small` `color.text.subtle`, the pairing axe's link-in-text-block flags, and asserts its underline at rest; contrast.test records that `color.text.brand` is not held against `color.text` or `color.text.subtle` because the underline is the non-colour cue. See Components/TextLink In Prose.
- **A Badge longer than its room ends in an ellipsis and reveals itself** (audit STS-3, G1-11). A Badge is never wider than its container (`max-w-full`); its text children sit in a Truncate, so a label cut by a status column ends in an ellipsis and shows whole on hover, and on keyboard focus of a badge that links, while it is cut. Icons stay direct children. A Badge with its own `title` shows that instead. Indicator's word and Person's name reveal the same way. See Components/Badge In Rows and Components/Indicator Truncation.
- **Only a Badge that links or acts answers the pointer** (audit STS-4, STS-5). The ghost and link treatments' hovers, like the filled and outline ones, apply to a Badge rendered as an anchor or a button only, and a Badge rendered as a button now takes the same hovers as a link. A brand badge link's hovered words are `color.text.selected` (5.2:1 and more), where `color.text.brand` was 4.18:1; contrast.test holds `color.text.brand` on `color.background.brand.subtlest`, the hovered pairing and the Avatar accents. See Components/Badge Hover Only When Interactive.
- **Count reads in the locale and signs a diff** (audit STS-7, STS-16, STS-17). A number Count is formatted with the provider's locale (1,189; 1.189 in German), its ceiling too ("9,999+"); an `added` Count carries a plus and a `removed` one a minus (U+2212), so the two differ by more than their fill, for a screen reader and in forced colours. Count.mdx states the zero rule as the parts apply it and names the parts that take a `count`. See Components/Count Count Matrix, Locale and In Context.
- **AvatarGroupCount fits every size and can open the rest** (audit STS-8, STS-15, STS-17). The +n circle takes its members' size and a type size under theirs, and the group's overlap scales with them (4px at xsmall to 16px at xlarge). `render` makes it a button, for a Popover or HoverCard that lists the members it stands for, with the focus ring and a touch hit area. Avatar's parts and Person now name themselves after the caller's props (`data-slot` last), AvatarBadge's docs keep presence out of the status tones, and Avatar.mdx has the family template with ArgTypes for every part. See Components/Avatar Group Sizes and Hidden Members.
- **Content parts keep their shapes in forced colours** (audit STS-14). Count, the avatar group's +n and a filled Avatar take a CanvasText hairline, the +n as a button a Highlight ring, and AvatarBadge's mark stays drawn. See Components/Count and Components/Avatar under the forced-colours project.
- **FilterChip names itself, and its docs drop the cycling toggle** (audit STS-10, STS-15). FilterChip puts `data-slot="filter-chip"` after the caller's props. FilterChip.mdx no longer recommends a toggle that cycles through values, and the In Toolbar story's Owner chip opens a Popover of radios, so no chip stays pressed while its name changes. See Components/FilterChip In Toolbar.
- **Id and Id.List take native props and a ref** (audit STS-15, STS-18, STS-20). Id forwards a span's props and ref and names itself `data-slot="id"`; Id.List does on its run (`data-slot="id-list"`, `data-empty` when there are none). Id.mdx no longer says an id is selected like a word; it says where to put a CopyButton instead. See Components/Id Native Attributes and Id Matrix.
- **Plays for the status parts** (audit STS-20). Count's cap, sign and locale; a Dot named as an image with a label and hidden without one, and an Indicator's own Dot hidden; Indicator truncation with its reveal; Id.List wrapping between ids and its empty word. See Components/Count, Indicator and Id.
- **Eyebrow, Fact and Fact.Group take native props and a ref; CodeBlock too** (audit CNT-16). Each names itself last with `data-slot` (`eyebrow`, `fact`, `fact-group`, `code-block`). A Fact's value and Prose's paragraphs break a long unbroken token where they must, so a URN never widens the strip or the column. See Components/Typography Eyebrow Native Attributes and Prose Long Content, and Components/Fact Long Value and Native Attributes.
- **CodeBlock takes a string, drops its gutter, and is a tab stop only while it scrolls** (audit CNT-13, CNT-14). `code` takes the text as one string split at its line breaks, in place of `lines` (now optional); `showLineNumbers={false}` leaves the gutter out, for a value that is not a file; the frame takes `tabIndex` only while it has more than it shows. CodeBlock.mdx drops the stale export-page claim. See Components/CodeBlock Value and Capped.
- **`IdList` and `FactGroup` are exported by name** (audit STS-15, CNT-16, CONTENT). `Id.List` and `Fact.Group` are unchanged; the named exports give their pages a props table. See Components/Id and Components/Fact.
- **A warning bar's fill is in the tone table** (audit G6-6, CONTENT). `toneClasses.warning.fill` is `bg-chart-warning`, the 3:1 fill Progress already drew, in place of the bold warning fill made to carry dark text, so the table's bar fill holds for every tone. See Components/Progress Tones.
- **Every content page has a Long content section** (audit G1-13, CONTENT). Badge, Count, Indicator, FilterChip, Id, TextLink, Typography, Fact, CodeBlock, Avatar, Attachment, DateTime and Diff say what each does with long text, by the rule in the component library guide's Long content section: titles, headings, errors, notifications and authored text wrap; ids, hashes and URLs wrap anywhere; only row fields truncate, always with a reveal; file names truncate in the middle. Truncate.mdx points at the rule and names the pages. See Components/Truncate.
- **A Dot is never the status alone** (audit STS-6, CONTENT). Indicator.mdx and Dot's docs no longer offer a bare Dot for a spot too small for a word: the Dot marks a status whose word is written beside it, and where there is no room for the word the status is an Indicator. A Dot's `label` names it for a screen reader only, the last resort, since a sighted reader still sees colour alone. The Don't story pairs an Indicator against a labelled Dot alone. See Components/Indicator Dont.
- **Badge's tone table is the kit's meanings** (audit STS-12, CONTENT). Badge.mdx's examples no longer place a product word the product map decides otherwise (In remediation), and the page says that a product's own status map, which follows these meanings, decides where each of its values falls. See Components/Badge Semantic Matrix.

- **Breaking: the 0.5.0 disclosure API is gone, with one exception to the alias rule** (audit DSC-14). `Collapsible.Group` has no alias: its children, a Collapsible with `title` and `count`, no longer exist, so an alias could not render them; `ledger/no-deprecated-name` names Accordion (`multiple`, root `defaultValue`, an AccordionItem per section). Collapsible's and Accordion's `title`, `count` and `headingLevel` are removed (the title and its Count are a CollapsibleHeader's or AccordionTrigger's children; the level is the heading-level context, a HeadingLevelProvider), and so is `DisclosureHeading`. The LegacyCollapsible and LegacyAccordion adapters do not ship. See Components/Collapsible, Migration and API.
- **Accordion parts take a state `className`, and the content's reaches the panel** (audit DSC-8). Accordion, AccordionItem, AccordionTrigger and AccordionContent keep Base UI's state function for `className` instead of dropping it, and AccordionContent's class goes to the panel that carries `data-open` and the measured height, not to the box inside it. AccordionTrigger takes `headerProps` for the heading around it (a class, attributes, a ref, `render` for another element) and `icon` to replace the chevron, `null` for none; its children are one title (`data-slot="accordion-trigger-title"`), as a CollapsibleHeader's are, so a Count runs on in the title's line. Inspector's data form no longer doubles the bottom space under a group. See Components/Accordion Parts.
- **Disclosure tints reach past a flush title** (audit DSC-16, REC-18). CollapsibleHeader (so Inspector.Group and a collapsible Section) and AccordionTrigger tint from `space.100` before the title, which stays flush with the rows under it, to the row's end, where the chevron now sits `space.100` inside; the tint reaches past the start only, where nothing can scroll to it. See Components/Collapsible Header and Components/Accordion Parts.
- **Nested disclosures fold on their own height** (audit DSC-11). Every kit disclosure panel names its measured height (`data-collapse-panel`) and the collapse keyframes read `--ds-collapse-height`, resolved on the panel itself, so a Collapsible inside an open Accordion item eases open instead of taking the item's `auto` and snapping. See Components/Collapsible Nested motion.
- **KeyValue labels wrap instead of being cut** (audit REC-17, CNT-2, VW3-9, VW2-11). A label longer than its column wraps to the next line, its first line on the value's baseline, so a touch reader reads the same label as everyone else; the page gives a label budget (about 14 characters at 104, 20 at 160) and says to widen the group rather than rely on the wrap. See Components/KeyValue Cut Value, Whole Label.
- **The KeyValue label column grows with the text** (audit CNT-2's text-size half, WCAG 1.4.4). `labelWidth` is still given in pixels at the default text size, and the column is now drawn in rem, so it grows with the reader's text size as the label and the value's 8rem do: 104 is 130 at a 20px root, and unchanged at 16px. See Components/KeyValue Group Layouts.
- **A KeyValue value leaves room for a focus ring** (audit EDT-1's KeyValue half, G3-11). Where the browser takes a clip margin, a truncating value clips on both axes with `space.050`, the ring's reach, all round: a TextLink or an Editable at either edge of the value keeps its whole ring, an open Editable's field its sides, and a 24px touch area still fits its 20px line; elsewhere the value clips across only, as before. A stacked KeyValue.Group row gives its label and value the row's width, so a long value truncates in it instead of running past the frame. See Components/KeyValue Ring Room, Control In A Value and Group Layouts.
- **Each Inspector group is one definition list** (audit REC-5, CNT-19, REC-22). Inspector's data form renders Inspector.Groups whose rows are one KeyValue.Group, so both forms share the header, the keyboard and the fold, and an Inspector.Group given only KeyValues makes them one KeyValue.Group, so a screen reader hears one list of pairs per group. See Patterns/Inspector Groups and Group.

- **Menu labels end in an ellipsis, never mid-word** (audit MNU-8, DTC-11's menu half, G1-4's menu part). A DropdownMenu item's words sit in one label (`data-slot="dropdown-menu-item-label"`) that ends in an ellipsis where the menu meets the window's edge, and a pointer resting on it shows the whole label; the accessible name stays whole. A `minWidth` passed in Content's `style` is capped at the available width, so it never pushes a menu off a phone. See Components/DropdownMenu Action Label Widths.
- **A shortcut is not part of an item's name** (audit MNU-15). `shortcut` on DropdownMenuItem and CommandItem, and on a PaletteCommand, takes the keys written once for every platform ("Mod+E"), draws them with KbdShortcut and sets `aria-keyshortcuts`, so the item is "Edit", not "Edit E". DropdownMenuShortcut and CommandShortcut are hidden from assistive technology; content at the end that a reader needs, such as a count, passes `aria-hidden={false}`. See Components/DropdownMenu Actions and Components/Command Filtering.
- **A DropdownMenuLabel outside a Group renders** (audit MNU-17). It names the Group or RadioGroup around it; outside one it renders in a group of its own and says once in the console that it names nothing, instead of throwing. See Components/DropdownMenu Label outside a group.
- **A chosen radio item carries a dot** (audit MNU-16's menu half). DropdownMenuRadioItem marks the chosen option with a dot and CheckboxItem an option that is on with a check, and DropdownMenu.mdx says to keep each kind in its own labelled Group apart from the actions. See Components/DropdownMenu Preferences.
- **Kbd names a glyph without `aria-label`** (audit MNU-11, G2-8). `label` on Kbd, and the new `label` on KbdGroup, hide the glyphs from assistive technology and read one visually hidden name in their place; an `aria-label` is read the same way and is no longer set on the `<kbd>`, which ARIA does not name. See Components/Kbd Kbd Matrix.
- **Command's active descendant follows the selection, and its count is spoken** (audit MNU-3, MNU-4, PIK-13's Command half). CommandInput names the selected row as `aria-activedescendant` on open, after each keystroke and after each arrow; Command says the count of matching rows ("1 match") or its empty sentence through the Announcer once the query holds still; a CommandInput hint other than the default describes the field. See Components/Command Filtering.
- **A load in Command is not an empty list** (audit MNU-6's kit half, PIK-13). CommandEmpty waits while a CommandLoading shows, and takes `noMatch` for a query that matches nothing, apart from a list with no rows. CommandLoading says Searching… by default. See Components/Command Loading.
- **Command speaks the locale** (audit MNU-13, PIK-13). CommandDialog's title and description, CommandCount's plural, CommandList's name ("Results", not cmdk's "Suggestions") and the palette's placeholder, empty sentence and footer verb come from the locale's messages; the field's Escape hint is one KbdShortcut. CommandDialog reads the palette's sentence only with the default title, so a picker titled "Link evidence" is no longer described as searching for a command. See Components/Command Palette.
- **The command and search fields show their focus, and short windows keep their rows** (audit G3-17, G5-11). The field's row in Command and SearchDialog draws the field's focus ring while the field has focus; CommandDialog and SearchDialog sit 80px from the top, or a tenth of the window where that is less. See Components/Command Short Viewport and Patterns/SearchDialog Search dialog.
- **CommandPalette matches labels, takes the caller's words, loads and fails** (audit MNU-2, MNU-5's kit half, MNU-6). Commands are scored on their label, heading and hint, never their id; `title`, `description`, `noResults` and `chooseHint` relabel it; `loading`, `error` and `onRetry` say Searching… or the failure with Try again instead of "No commands match.". See Patterns/CommandPalette Matches labels, not ids, Loading and failure, and Failure.
- **The command row Enter would choose draws the focus outline** (audit A11-2, TOK-7, MENUS). Focus stays in a Command's field, so its selected row, the field's active descendant, draws the field's focus outline over its tint while the field has focus, as Combobox and SearchDialog rows do, and gives it up when focus leaves; the row under the pointer keeps the tint alone. See Components/Command Filtering.
- **CommandCount's own words keep the locale's plural** (audit MNU-13, MENUS). `one` or `many` alone no longer falls back to English: the word left out is the locale's, in its plural rules. See Components/Command Count words.
- **The palette draws its shortcuts with CommandShortcut** (audit MNU-15, MENUS). A command's `shortcut` sits beside its `hint` in the kit's hidden CommandShortcut, so the row's name is its label and its keys are its `aria-keyshortcuts`. See Patterns/CommandPalette Command Palette Matrix.
- **CommandLoading holds the empty sentence back from the first frame** (audit MNU-6, MENUS). It registers before the first paint, so CommandEmpty never shows for a frame while rows load. See Components/Command Loading.
- **useCommandPalette's shortcut follows the platform and stays out of the way** (audit MNU-12). ⌘K on Apple platforms and Ctrl+K elsewhere, ignored on a held key's repeats, with another modifier held, while composing, when a handler has taken the key and while another dialog is open; `useCommandPalette({ shortcut })` changes it or turns it off. See Patterns/CommandPalette Command palette.
- **DropdownMenu says when to use it** (audit MNU-20, MNU-10's kit half). DropdownMenu.mdx gains When to use and Content sections, including the trailing ChevronDown on a labelled trigger; CommandPalette.mdx's Style table matches the code. `variant="destructive"` renders as `danger` for one version; write `danger`. See Components/DropdownMenu Action Label Widths and Descriptions and disabled reasons, whose disabled row's reason is checked in forced colours too.

- **The dialog under a decision dims** (audit MOD-8, VW3-7). An AlertDialog rendered inside the Dialog or Sheet that owns the decision (Base UI draws no second blanket for it) dims its parent under `color.blanket`, fading on `motion.duration.medium` and at once under reduced motion, so the parent's primary reads as out of play and the prompt stands apart in dark mode; the layer covers whatever part of the parent shows, however far a short window has scrolled it. See Components/Dialog Form and Components/Overlays Stacked, in both modes.
- **Close comes first in the Tab order** (audit MOD-12). The close button of DialogContent and SheetContent is first in the DOM, where it is drawn, so Shift+Tab from the first field reaches it and Tab from the footer wraps to it; without `initialFocus` the first control after it still takes focus, and a touch screen still focuses the popup. `DialogFooter showCloseButton` puts its subtle Close before the children, in Cancel's place (MOD-13). See Components/Dialog Form and Scrollable.
- **Focus returns to the opener of a dialog opened from state** (audit MOD-1's kit half). Dialog, Sheet and AlertDialog record the element that had focus as `open` turns true, before a field inside can take focus as it mounts, and return focus there on close while it is on the page and enabled; `finalFocus` still wins. A press on a blanket that leaves its overlay open (an AlertDialog's, or a `pending` or `disablePointerDismissal` one's) keeps focus where it was instead of dropping it to the page. See Components/Dialog Focus Return From State and Pending And Nested Popup, and Components/AlertDialog Confirmation And Pending.
- **Overlays keep their edge in forced colours** (audit MOD-9, FLT-3, TOK-4's overlay half).
